# Preface<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001664984038"></a>

**Overview<a name="section4537382116410"></a>**

This document mainly describes BS2X device driver development, including working principles, interface usage described by scenario, and precautions.

This document uses BS21 as an example for illustration. It will not be explained separately later. Please be aware of this.

All driver interfaces return a value. When using them, it is recommended that you check the return value of each interface to determine whether the execution succeeded. For the error codes of return values, see "include/errcode.h".

# Pinctrl<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001665143778"></a>






## Overview<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713103581"></a>

The Pinctrl controller is provided to control the multiplexing function of IO pins. The configurable specifications are as follows:

-   Supports configuring 32 IO pins.
-   Supports configuring IO drive strength, IO function multiplexing, and setting IO pull-up/pull-down states.

## Function Description<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001665143782"></a>

The interfaces and functions provided by the Pinctrl driver module are as follows:

-   uapi\_pin\_init(void): Initializes Pinctrl.
-   uapi\_pin\_deinit(void): Deinitializes Pinctrl.
-   uapi\_pin\_set\_mode(pin\_t pin, pin\_mode\_t mode): Sets the multiplexing mode of the specified IO (pin indicates the current IO pin, and mode indicates the multiplexing mode to be configured for the current pin).
-   uapi\_pin\_get\_mode(pin\_t pin): Gets the multiplexing mode of the specified IO (pin indicates the current IO pin).
-   uapi\_pin\_set\_ds(pin\_t pin, pin\_drive\_strength\_t ds): Sets the drive strength of the specified IO (pin indicates the current IO pin, and ds indicates the drive strength to be configured for the current pin).
-   uapi\_pin\_get\_ds(pin\_t pin): Gets the drive strength of the specified IO (pin indicates the current IO pin).
-   uapi\_pin\_set\_pull(pin\_t pin, pin\_pull\_t pull\_type): Sets the pull-up/pull-down state of the specified IO (pin indicates the current IO pin, and pull\_type indicates the pull-up/pull-down state to be configured for the current pin).
-   uapi\_pin\_get\_pull(pin\_t pin): Gets the pull-up/pull-down state of the specified IO (pin indicates the current IO pin).
-   uapi\_pin\_set\_ie(pin\_t pin, pin\_input\_enable\_t ie): Sets the IE enable/disable of the specified IO (pin indicates the current IO pin, and ie indicates the input interrupt enable state to be configured for the current pin). (It is available only when the CONFIG\_PINCTRL\_SUPPORT\_IE macro is enabled)
-   uapi\_pin\_get\_ie(pin\_t pin): Gets the IE enable/disable of the specified IO (pin indicates the current IO pin). (It is available only when the CONFIG\_PINCTRL\_SUPPORT\_IE macro is enabled)

## Development Guide<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001665143786"></a>

The usage of the Pinctrl interface follows the steps below (the following steps are optional based on actual needs):

1.  Call the uapi\_pin\_init interface to initialize the PINCTRL module.
2.  Call the uapi\_pin\_set\_ie and uapi\_pin\_get\_ie interfaces to set/query the IE input interrupt enable/disable of the specified IO.
3.  Call the uapi\_pin\_set\_mode and uapi\_pin\_get\_mode interfaces to set/query the multiplexing mode of the specified IO.
4.  Call the uapi\_pin\_set\_ds and uapi\_pin\_get\_ds interfaces to set/query the drive strength of the specified IO.
5.  Call the uapi\_pin\_set\_pull and uapi\_pin\_get\_pull interfaces to set/query the pull-up/pull-down state of the specified IO.
6.  Call the uapi\_pin\_deinit interface to deinitialize the PINCTRL module.

Example:

```
    /* Initialize the PINCTRL module */ 
    uapi_pin_init();
    /* Enable the IE of S_MGPIO19 */
    uapi_pin_set_ie(S_MGPIO19, PIN_IE_1);
    /* Set the multiplexing function of S_MGPIO19 to GPIO */
    uapi_pin_set_mode(S_MGPIO19, HAL_PIO_FUNC_GPIO);
    /* Get the multiplexing function of S_MGPIO19 (GPIO) */
    pin_mode_t mode = uapi_pin_get_mode(S_MGPIO19);
    /* Set the drive strength of S_MGPIO19 to PIN_DS_2 */
    uapi_pin_set_ds(S_MGPIO19, PIN_DS_2);
    /* Get the drive strength of S_MGPIO19 (PIN_DS_2) */
    pin_drive_strength_t ds = uapi_pin_get_ds(S_MGPIO19);
    /* Set S_MGPIO19 to pull-up mode */
    uapi_pin_set_pull(S_MGPIO19, PIN_PULL_UP);
    /* Get S_MGPIO19 (pull-up mode) */
    pin_pull_t pull = uapi_pin_get_pull(S_MGPIO19);
    /* Deinitialize the PINCTRL module */ 
    uapi_pin_deinit();
```

## Precautions<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001664984034"></a>

1.  When configuring the IO multiplexing function, pay attention to whether the IO supports the target function or has been multiplexed to other functions, to avoid affecting existing functions. For IO multiplexing, refer to the definition of the "pin\_mode\_t" structure in the source code "sdk\\drivers\\chips\\bs2x\\porting\\pinctrl\\pinctrl\_porting.h".

1.  By default, the SDK configures the IE of all pins as enabled. Generally, for pins on which interrupts may occur or which are responsible for data transmission, IE must be enabled to avoid affecting the IP functions. For other pins, IE can be disabled to avoid unnecessary IO leakage current that affects power consumption.

    This product has configured the pins that require IE enablement in the corresponding drivers.

    Configuration process: Use the function uapi\_pin\_set\_ie\(pin, PIN\_IE\_0/PIN\_IE\_1\) to disable/enable IE, and ensure that the CONFIG\_PINCTRL\_SUPPORT\_IE macro is enabled.

2.  PIN\_DS\_0 provides the weakest drive strength, and PIN\_DS\_3 provides the strongest.

>![](public_sys-resources/icon-note.gif) **Note:** 
>If IE is not enabled but a GPIO low-level interrupt is registered, the CPU will keep receiving interrupts.
>IE controls the input interrupt enable function. If GPIO is configured to output a high level, it is recommended to configure Pinctrl pull-up; otherwise, leakage current may occur.

# GPIO<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001664984030"></a>






## Overview<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713023593"></a>

GPIO (General-purpose input/output) is the abbreviation for general-purpose input/output, a universal I/O interface standard. It can be configured as input or output mode to control external devices or communicate with other devices. It can be used to connect various devices, such as LED lights, sensors, actuators, and so on.

The GPIO specifications are as follows:

-   Supports setting/getting the GPIO pin direction and setting/getting the output level state.
-   Supports external level interrupt and external edge interrupt reporting.
-   Supports an independent interrupt for each GPIO.

## Function Description<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713023597"></a>

The interfaces and functions provided by the GPIO module are as follows:

-   uapi\_gpio\_init(void): Initializes GPIO.
-   uapi\_gpio\_deinit(void): Deinitializes GPIO.
-   uapi\_gpio\_set\_dir(pin\_t pin, gpio\_direction\_t dir): Sets the direction (input/output) of the specified GPIO (pin indicates the current IO pin, and dir indicates the input/output direction to be configured for the current pin).
-   uapi\_gpio\_get\_dir(pin\_t pin): Gets the direction (input/output) of the specified GPIO (pin indicates the current IO pin).
-   uapi\_gpio\_set\_val(pin\_t pin, gpio\_level\_t level): Sets the output level state of the specified GPIO (pin indicates the current IO pin, and level indicates the level state to be configured for the current pin).
-   uapi\_gpio\_get\_val(pin\_t pin): Gets the input level state of the specified GPIO (pin indicates the current IO pin).
-   uapi\_gpio\_get\_output\_val(pin\_t pin): Gets the output level state of the specified GPIO (pin indicates the current IO pin).
-   uapi\_gpio\_toggle(pin\_t pin): Toggles the output level state of GPIO (pin indicates the current IO pin).
-   uapi\_gpio\_register\_isr\_func(pin\_t pin, uint32\_t trigger, gpio\_callback\_t callback): Registers the interrupt of the specified GPIO (pin indicates the current IO pin, trigger indicates the interrupt type to be configured for the current pin, and callback indicates the callback function configured for the current pin).
-   uapi\_gpio\_unregister\_isr\_func(pin\_t pin): Unregisters the interrupt of the specified GPIO (pin indicates the current IO pin).
-   uapi\_gpio\_enable\_interrupt(pin\_t pin): Enables the GPIO interrupt (pin indicates the current IO pin).
-   uapi\_gpio\_disable\_interrupt(pin\_t pin): Disables the GPIO interrupt (pin indicates the current IO pin).
-   uapi\_gpio\_clear\_interrupt(pin\_t pin): Clears the GPIO interrupt (pin indicates the current IO pin).

<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="11.338866113388663%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.5 "><p id="p194051046681"><a name="p194051046681"></a><a name="p194051046681"></a>No</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="14.398560143985604%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.6 "><p id="p154053469813"><a name="p154053469813"></a><a name="p154053469813"></a>Disabled by default. It is not used in this product. It is recommended to disable it.</p>
</td>
</tr>

## Development Guide<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713103585"></a>

The GPIO interface usage follows these steps:

1.  Call the uapi\_pin\_init and uapi\_gpio\_init interfaces to initialize the PINCTRL and GPIO modules.
2.  Call the uapi\_pin\_set\_mode interface to set the PIN mux to the GPIO function.
3.  Based on development requirements, the GPIO interface can be set to output, input, and interrupt modes as follows:
    -   Output mode:
        1.  Call the uapi\_gpio\_set\_dir interface to set the GPIO direction to OUT. You can call the uapi\_gpio\_get\_dir interface to check whether the GPIO direction is set correctly.
        2.  Call the uapi\_gpio\_set\_val interface to set the GPIO output level (high/low).

    -   Input mode:
        1.  Call the uapi\_gpio\_set\_dir interface to set the GPIO direction to IN. You can call the uapi\_gpio\_get\_dir interface to check whether the GPIO direction is set correctly.
        2.  Call the uapi\_pin\_set\_pull interface to configure pull up, pull down, or pull none.
        3.  Call the uapi\_gpio\_get\_val interface to obtain the GPIO input level.

    -   Interrupt mode:
        1.  Call the uapi\_gpio\_set\_dir interface to set the GPIO direction to IN. You can call the uapi\_gpio\_get\_dir interface to check whether the GPIO direction is set correctly.
        2.  Call the uapi\_gpio\_register\_isr\_func interface to register the GPIO interrupt callback function.
        3.  Call the uapi\_gpio\_enable\_interrupt interface to enable the GPIO interrupt.
        4.  Call the uapi\_gpio\_unregister\_isr\_func interface to unregister the GPIO interrupt callback function (called when deregistering the interrupt).
        5.  Call the uapi\_gpio\_disable\_interrupt interface to disable the GPIO interrupt.
        6.  Call the uapi\_gpio\_clear\_interrupt interface to clear the GPIO interrupt.

4.  Call the uapi\_gpio\_deinit interface to deinitialize the GPIO module.

The following uses input mode as an example:

```
#include "gpio.h"
void gpio_callback_func(pin_t pin, uintptr_t param)
{
    unused(param);
    uapi_gpio_disable_interrupt(pin);
    osal_printk("PIN:%d interrupt success. \r\n", pin);
}
errcode_t sample_gpio_test(pin_t pin)
{
    uapi_pin_init();
    uapi_gpio_init();
    uapi_pin_set_mode(pin, HAL_PIO_FUNC_GPIO); /* Set the specified IO mux to GPIO mode */
    uapi_gpio_set_dir(pin, GPIO_DIRECTION_INPUT); /* Set the specified GPIO to input mode */
    /* Register the rising-edge interrupt of the specified GPIO with gpio_callback_func as the callback */
    if (uapi_gpio_register_isr_func(pin, GPIO_INTERRUPT_RISING_EDGE, gpio_callback_func) != ERRCODE_SUCC) {
        uapi_gpio_unregister_isr_func(pin); /* Clear the residual state */
        return ERRCODE_FAIL;
    }
    uapi_gpio_enable_interrupt(pin)；
    return ERRCODE_SUCC;
}
```

## Precautions<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713103577"></a>

-   When using the GPIO level interrupt, clear the interrupt and temporarily disable the level trigger in the callback function. Otherwise, the system will stay in interrupt handling and cannot execute other functions.
-   When there is no explicit requirement, it is recommended to use the default configuration for the trigger.

# UART<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001665143766"></a>






## Overview<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001665143762"></a>

UART (Universal Asynchronous Receiver/Transmitter) is a serial, asynchronous, full-duplex communication protocol used for data transmission between devices. It is one of the most commonly used communication protocols between devices. When properly configured, UART can work with many different types of serial protocols that involve sending and receiving serial data.

The MCU side of the chip provides three configurable UART peripheral units: UART0 (UART\_L0), UART1 (UART\_H0), and UART2 (UART\_L1).

The UART specifications are as follows:

-   Programmable data bits, stop bits, and parity bits are supported.
-   UART supports the flow control working mode and RTS waterline configuration.
-   TX and RX FIFOs of 64x8 are provided.
-   Interrupt masking and response for receive FIFO, transmit FIFO, receive timeout, and error interrupts are supported.
-   The DMA data transfer mode is supported.

## Function Description<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001664984006"></a>

>![](public_sys-resources/icon-note.gif) **Note:** 
>If the UART driver needs to support DMA data transmission and reception, ensure that the DMA driver has been initialized. For DMA initialization, refer to “[DMA](DMA.md)”.

The driver code declares UART driver functions in include\\driver\\uart.h. The interfaces and functions provided are as follows:

-   uapi\_uart\_init（uart\_bus\_t bus, const uart\_pin\_config\_t \*pins, const uart\_attr\_t \*attr, const uart\_extra\_attr\_t \*extra\_attr, uart\_buffer\_config\_t \*uart\_buffer\_config）：Initialize the UART module（bus indicates the current UART bus number，pins indicates the PINs used by UART, including TX, RX, RTS, and CTS，attr indicates the basic configuration parameters of UART，extra\_attr indicates the advanced configuration parameters of UART，uart\_buffer\_config indicates the receive buffer specified for UART）。
-   uapi\_uart\_deinit（uart\_bus\_t bus）：Deinitialize UART（bus indicates the current UART bus number）。
-   uapi\_uart\_set\_attr（uart\_bus\_t bus, const uart\_attr\_t \*attr）：Set the UART configuration parameters（bus indicates the current UART bus number，attr indicates the basic configuration parameters of UART）。
-   uapi\_uart\_get\_attr（uart\_bus\_t bus, const uart\_attr\_t \*attr）：Get the UART configuration parameters（bus indicates the current UART bus number，attr indicates the basic configuration parameters of UART）。
-   uapi\_uart\_has\_pending\_transmissions（uart\_bus\_t bus）：Query whether UART is transmitting data（bus indicates the current UART bus number）。
-   uapi\_uart\_rx\_fifo\_is\_empty（uart\_bus\_t bus）：Check whether the RX FIFO is empty（bus indicates the current UART bus number）。
-   uapi\_uart\_tx\_fifo\_is\_empty（uart\_bus\_t bus）：Check whether the TX FIFO is empty（bus indicates the current UART bus number）。
-   uapi\_uart\_write（uart\_bus\_t bus, const uint8\_t \*buffer, uint32\_t length, uint32\_t timeout）：Send data to the opened UART in direct transmission mode（bus indicates the current UART bus number，buffer indicates the buffer for data to be sent，length indicates the length of the buffer for data to be sent，timeout indicates the timeout period. This parameter is not used for the time being.(CONFIG\_SUPPORT\_UART\_TX\_POLL\_TIMEOUT must be enabled for the timeout parameter to take effect)）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_UART\_SUPPORT\_TX macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_uart\_write\_nolock（uart\_bus\_t bus, const uint8\_t \*buffer, uint32\_t length, uint32\_t timeout）：Send data to the opened UART without locking interrupts in direct transmission mode（bus indicates the current UART bus number，buffer indicates the buffer for data to be sent，length indicates the length of the buffer for data to be sent，timeout indicates the timeout period. This parameter is not used for the time being.(CONFIG\_SUPPORT\_UART\_TX\_POLL\_TIMEOUT must be enabled for the timeout parameter to take effect)）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_UART\_SUPPORT\_TX macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_uart\_write\_int（uart\_bus\_t bus, const uint8\_t \*buffer, uint32\_t length, void \*params, uart\_tx\_callback\_t finished\_with\_buffer\_func）：Send data to the opened UART in interrupt mode. When data transmission is complete, the callback function is called（bus indicates the current UART bus number，buffer indicates the buffer for data to be sent，length indicates the length of the buffer for data to be sent，params indicates the parameters passed to the callback function when transmission is complete，finished\_with\_buffer\_func indicates the callback function called after data sending is complete）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_UART\_SUPPORT\_TX macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_uart\_write\_by\_dma（uart\_bus\_t bus, const void \*buffer, uint32\_t length, uart\_write\_dma\_config\_t \*dma\_cfg）：Send data through DMA（bus indicates the current UART bus number，buffer indicates the buffer for data to be sent，length indicates the length of the buffer for data to be sent，dma\_cfg indicates the DMA configuration for sending data）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_UART\_SUPPORT\_TX and CONFIG\_UART\_SUPPORT\_DMA macros are enabled.)
-   uapi\_uart\_read\_by\_dma（uart\_bus\_t bus, const void \*buffer, uint32\_t length, uart\_write\_dma\_config\_t \*dma\_cfg）：Receive data through DMA（bus indicates the current UART bus number，buffer indicates the buffer for storing received data，length indicates the length of the buffer for storing received data，dma\_cfg indicates the DMA configuration for receiving data）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_UART\_SUPPORT\_TX and CONFIG\_UART\_SUPPORT\_DMA macros are enabled.)
-   uapi\_uart\_register\_read\_by\_dma\_callback（uart\_bus\_t bus, uart\_write\_dma\_config\_t \*dma\_cfg）：Register the callback for triggering DMA transfer by interrupt（bus indicates the current UART bus number，dma\_cfg indicates the DMA configuration for receiving data）。

    (It can be used only when the CONFIG\_UART\_SUPPORT\_TX, CONFIG\_UART\_SUPPORT\_DMA, and CONFIG\_UART\_SUPPORT\_INT\_TRIGGER\_DMA macros are enabled.)

-   uapi\_uart\_unregister\_read\_by\_dma\_callback（uart\_bus\_t bus）：Deregister the callback for triggering DMA transfer by interrupt（bus indicates the current UART bus number）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_UART\_SUPPORT\_TX, CONFIG\_UART\_SUPPORT\_DMA, and CONFIG\_UART\_SUPPORT\_INT\_TRIGGER\_DMA macros are enabled.)
-   uapi\_uart\_register\_write\_by\_dma\_callback（uart\_bus\_t bus, uart\_tx\_by\_dma\_callback\_t tx\_dma\_cb）：Register the callback function that directly calls DMA write in the DMA interrupt（bus indicates the current UART bus number，tx\_dma\_cb indicates the callback function that calls DMA write in the DMA completion interrupt）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_UART\_SUPPORT\_SEND\_IN\_DMA\_ISR macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_uart\_register\_rx\_callback（uart\_bus\_t bus, uart\_rx\_condition\_t condition, uint32\_t size, uart\_rx\_callback\_t callback）：Register the receive callback function, which is triggered based on the trigger condition and size（bus indicates the current UART bus number，condition indicates the condition for triggering the callback，size indicates the required data length when the trigger condition involves the data length，callback indicates the callback function called after data is received）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_UART\_SUPPORT\_RX macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_uart\_unregister\_rx\_callback（uart\_bus\_t bus）：Cancel the registration of the receive callback function（bus indicates the current UART bus number）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_UART\_SUPPORT\_RX macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_uart\_register\_parity\_error\_callback（uart\_bus\_t bus, uart\_error\_callback\_t callback）：Register the callback function for parity error handling（bus indicates the current UART bus number，callback indicates the callback function for parity error handling）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_UART\_SUPPORT\_RX macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_uart\_register\_frame\_error\_callback（uart\_bus\_t bus, uart\_error\_callback\_t callback）：Register the callback function for frame error handling（bus indicates the current UART bus number，callback indicates the callback function for frame error handling）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_UART\_SUPPORT\_RX macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_uart\_register\_overrun\_error\_callback（uart\_bus\_t bus, uart\_error\_callback\_t callback）：Register the callback function for overrun error handling（bus indicates the current UART bus number，callback indicates the callback function for overrun error handling）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_UART\_SUPPORT\_RX macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_uart\_read（uart\_bus\_t bus, const uint8\_t \*buffer, uint32\_t length, uint32\_t timeout）：Read data from UART（bus indicates the current UART bus number，buffer indicates the buffer for storing received data，length indicates the length of the buffer for storing received data，timeout indicates the timeout period. This parameter is not used for the time being.）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_UART\_SUPPORT\_RX macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_uart\_flush\_rx\_data（uart\_bus\_t bus）：Flush the data in the UART receive buffer（bus indicates the current UART bus number）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_UART\_SUPPORT\_RX macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_uart\_update\_rx\_buffer（uart\_bus\_t bus, uint8\_t \*rx\_buffer, uint16\_t rx\_buffer\_size）：Update the address and length of the UART receive buffer（bus indicates the current UART bus number，rx\_buffer indicates the buffer for storing received data，length indicates the length of the buffer for storing received data）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_UART\_SUPPORT\_RX macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_uart\_suspend（uintptr\_t arg）：Suspend all UART channels for low-power scenarios（arg indicates the parameters required for suspension）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_UART\_SUPPORT\_LPM macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_uart\_resume（uintptr\_t arg）：Resume all UART channels for low-power scenarios（arg indicates the parameters required for resume）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_UART\_SUPPORT\_LPM macro is enabled.)
-   uart\_port\_lli\_init\_config：Interface for configuring the pins required by UART. The input parameter is the UART channel required for the test.
-   uapi\_uart\_dma\_send: An interface for sending data through the UART-DMA circular linked list. The input parameters include the UART used for the test, the buffer address of the data to be sent, the length of the data, and the callback function after data sending. The callback function type is typedef void (*uart\_data\_send\_cb\_t)(uint8\_t channel, errcode\_t result). errcode\_t result returns the type of error that occurs during data sending. This interface sends data once per call, and multi-packet transmission requires repeated calls. Whether to send the packet header can be controlled through the Kconfig option (Top) -> Drivers -> Drivers -> UART -> Uart Configuration -> Whether DMA need send head or not. If packets of variable length are to be sent, the packet header must be sent. If packets of fixed length are to be sent, the data length can be confirmed in the DMA initialization configuration.
-   uapi\_uart\_dma\_recv\_register: An interface for initializing reception through UART-DMA. The input parameters include the UART used for the test, the buffer address for receiving data, the length of the data to be received, and the callback function after data reception. The callback function type is typedef bool (*uart\_data\_recv\_cb\_t)(uint8\_t channel, uint16\_t length, errcode\_t result). The Boolean return value tells the driver whether it can continue receiving, and errcode\_t result returns the type of error that occurs during data reception. For data reception, you only need to register the callback and reception is automatic, so the interface only needs to be called once.
-   uapi\_uart\_dma\_recv\_register: An interface for resuming UART-DMA circular linked-list reception. The receiver can return false in the callback function of the uapi\_uart\_dma\_recv\_register function to pause reception, and call uapi\_uart\_dma\_lli\_continue\_recv to resume reception when needed.
-   uapi\_uart\_update\_dma\_recv\_buff: An interface for switching the receive buffer address of the UART-DMA circular linked list. The receiver can use this interface to implement functions such as a ping-pong buffer.

This macro takes effect only when the flow_ctrl parameter is set to 3 when calling the initialization interface; otherwise, it is useless.</p>

## Development Guide<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001664984014"></a>

The following uses UART1 as an example. The data transmission and reception process is as follows:

1.  Configure the IO mux. Set the corresponding IOs to the TX, RX, RTS, and CTS functions of UART1.

    If hardware flow control is not required, configure only TX and RX.

    ```
    void usr_uart_io_config(void)
    {
        /* The following IO mux configuration can also be centralized in the usr_io_init function in the SDK */
        uapi_pin_set_mode(S_AGPIO5, HAL_PIO_UART_H0_RTS); /* uart1 rts */
        uapi_pin_set_mode(S_AGPIO6, HAL_PIO_UART_H0_CTS); /* uart1 cts */
        uapi_pin_set_mode(S_AGPIO12, HAL_PIO_UART_H0_TXD); /* uart1 tx */
        uapi_pin_set_mode(S_AGPIO13, HAL_PIO_UART_H0_RXD); /* uart1 rx */
    }
    ```

2.  Initialize UART. Configure attributes such as the baud rate and data bits of UART, and enable UART.

    ```
    #define TEST_UART_RX_BUFF_SIZE　0x40
    unsigned char g_uart_rx_buff[TEST_UART_RX_BUFF_SIZE] = { 0 };
    uart_buffer_config_t g_uart_buffer_config = {
        .rx_buffer = g_uart_rx_buff,
        .rx_buffer_size = TEST_UART_RX_BUFF_SIZE
    };
    errcode_t usr_uart_init_config(void)
    {
        errcode_t errcode;
        uart_attr_t attr = {
            .baud_rate = 115200, /* Baud rate */
            .data_bits = 3,      /* 8-bit data bits */
            .stop_bits = 0,      /* 1-bit stop bit */
            .parity = 0,         /* Parity bit */
            .flow_ctrl = 0       /* Flow control */
        };
        uart_pin_config_t pin_config = {
            .tx_pin = S_AGPIO12, /* uart1 tx */
            .rx_pin = S_AGPIO13, /* uart1 rx */
            .cts_pin = S_AGPIO6, /* Flow control function, optional */
            .rts_pin = S_AGPIO5  /* Flow control function, optional */
        };
        uapi_uart_deinit(UART_BUS_1);   /* The deinitialization here is intended to clear some configurations used elsewhere previously to avoid interference */
        errcode = uapi_uart_init(UART_BUS_1, &pin_config, &attr, NULL, &g_uart_buffer_config);
        if (errcode != ERRCODE_SUCC) {
            osal_printk("uart init fail\r\n");
        }
        return errcode;
    }
    ```

3.  Send and receive UART data. Call the UART polling read/write interfaces to send and receive data.

    ```
    void usr_uart_read_data(void)
    {
        int len;
        unsigned char g_test_uart_rx_buffer[64];
        len = uapi_uart_read(UART_BUS_1, g_test_uart_rx_buffer, 64, 0);
        if(len > 0) {
            /* process */
        }
    }
    int usr_uart_write_data(unsigned int size, char* buff)
    {
        unsigned char tx_buff[64] = { 0 };
        if (memcpy_s(tx_buff, 64, buff, size) != EOK) {
            return ERRCODE_FAIL;
        }
        int ret = uapi_uart_write(UART_BUS_1, tx_buff, size, 0);
        if(ret == -1) {
            return ERRCODE_FAIL;
        }
        return ERRCODE_SUCC;
    }
    ```

The process of sending data in UART DMA mode is as follows:

1.  Configure the IO mux. Set the corresponding IOs to the TX, RX, RTS, and CTS functions of UART1.

    If hardware flow control is not required, configure only TX and RX.

    ```
    void usr_uart_io_config(void)
    {
        /* The following IO mux configuration can also be centralized in the usr_io_init function in the SDK */
        uapi_pin_set_mode(S_MGPIO21, HAL_PIO_UART_H0_RTS); /* uart1 rts */
        uapi_pin_set_mode(S_MGPIO22, HAL_PIO_UART_H0_CTS); /* uart1 cts */
        uapi_pin_set_mode(S_MGPIO17, HAL_PIO_UART_H0_TXD); /* uart1 tx */
        uapi_pin_set_mode(S_MGPIO18, HAL_PIO_UART_H0_RXD); /* uart1 rx */
    }
    ```

2.  Initialize UART. Configure attributes such as the baud rate and data bits of UART, and enable UART.

    ```
    errcode_t usr_uart_init_config(void)
    {
        errcode_t errcode;
        uart_attr_t attr = {
            .baud_rate = 115200, /* Baud rate */
            .data_bits = 3,      /* 8-bit data bits */
            .stop_bits = 0,      /* 1-bit stop bit */
            .parity = 0,         /* Parity bit */
            .flow_ctrl = 0       /* Flow control */
        };
        uart_pin_config_t pin_config = {
            .tx_pin = S_MGPIO17, /* uart1 tx */
            .rx_pin = S_MGPIO18, /* uart1 rx */
            .cts_pin = S_MGPIO22, /* Flow control function, optional */
            .rts_pin = S_MGPIO21/* Flow control function, optional */
        };
        uart_extra_attr_t ext_config = {
            .tx_dma_enable = true,
            .tx_int_threshold = UART_FIFO_INT_TX_LEVEL_EQ_0_CHARACTER,
            .rx_dma_enable = true,
            .rx_int_threshold = UART_FIFO_INT_RX_LEVEL_1_CHARACTER
        }
        errcode = uapi_uart_init(UART_BUS_1, &pin_config, &attr, &ext_config, &g_uart_buffer_config);
        if (errcode != ERRCODE_SUCC) {
            osal_printk("uart init fail\r\n");
        }
        return errcode;
    }
    ```

3.  Send and receive UART data through DMA.

    ```
    #define TEST_UART_DMA_SEND_BUFF_SIZE 1024
    
    
    static errcode_t test_uart_read_by_dma(void)
    {
        int len;
        unsigned char g_test_uart_rx_buffer[TEST_UART_DMA_SEND_BUFF_SIZE] = { 0 };
        uart_write_dma_config_t dma_cfg = {
            .src_width = HAL_DMA_TRANSFER_WIDTH_8,              /* 0 indicates 8-bit */
            .dest_width = HAL_DMA_TRANSFER_WIDTH_8,             /* 0 indicates 8-bit */
            .burst_length = HAL_DMA_BURST_TRANSACTION_LENGTH_1, /* Indicates 1 byte */
            .priority = 0                                       /* Priority 0 */
        };
        len = uapi_uart_read_by_dma(UART_BUS_1, g_test_uart_rx_buffer, TEST_UART_DMA_SEND_BUFF_SIZE, &dma_cfg);
        if(len > 0) {
            /* process */
        }
    }
    static errcode_t test_uart_write_by_dma(void)
    {
        uint8_t dma_buff[TEST_UART_DMA_SEND_BUFF_SIZE] = { 0 };
        if (memset_s(dma_buff, TEST_UART_DMA_SEND_BUFF_SIZE, 0XA5, TEST_UART_DMA_SEND_BUFF_SIZE) != 0) {
            return ERRCODE_FAIL;
        }
        uart_write_dma_config_t dma_cfg = {
            .src_width = HAL_DMA_TRANSFER_WIDTH_8,              /* 0 indicates 8-bit */
            .dest_width = HAL_DMA_TRANSFER_WIDTH_8,             /* 0 indicates 8-bit */
            .burst_length = HAL_DMA_BURST_TRANSACTION_LENGTH_1, /* Indicates 1 byte */
            .priority = 0                                       /* Priority 0 */
        };
        if (uapi_uart_write_by_dma(UART_BUS_1, dma_buff, len, &dma_cfg) != len) {
            osal_printk("[UART] *** memory to uart fail!\r\n");
            return ERRCODE_FAIL;
        }
        return ERRCODE_SUCC;
    }
    ```

For the complete code of UART transmission and reception in the three modes (polling, interrupt, and DMA), see the following code:

```
#include "pinctrl.h"
#include "uart.h"
#include "watchdog.h"
#include "soc_osal.h"
#include "app_init.h"
#if defined(CONFIG_UART_SUPPORT_DMA)
#include "dma.h"
#include "hal_dma.h"
#endif

#define UART_BAUDRATE                      115200
#define CONFIG_UART_INT_WAIT_MS            5

#define UART_TASK_PRIO                     24
#define UART_TASK_STACK_SIZE               0x1000

static uint8_t g_app_uart_rx_buff[CONFIG_UART_TRANSFER_SIZE] = { 0 };
#if defined(CONFIG_UART_SUPPORT_INT_MODE)
static uint8_t g_app_uart_int_rx_flag = 0;
static volatile uint8_t g_app_uart_int_index = 0;
static uint8_t g_app_uart_int_rx_buff[CONFIG_UART_TRANSFER_SIZE] = { 0 };
#endif
static uart_buffer_config_t g_app_uart_buffer_config = {
    .rx_buffer = g_app_uart_rx_buff,
    .rx_buffer_size = CONFIG_UART_TRANSFER_SIZE
};

#if defined(CONFIG_UART_SUPPORT_DMA)
uart_write_dma_config_t g_app_dma_cfg = {
    .src_width = HAL_DMA_TRANSFER_WIDTH_8,
    .dest_width = HAL_DMA_TRANSFER_WIDTH_8,
    .burst_length = HAL_DMA_BURST_TRANSACTION_LENGTH_1,
    .priority = HAL_DMA_CH_PRIORITY_0
};
#endif

static void app_uart_init_pin(void)
{
#if defined(CONFIG_PINCTRL_SUPPORT_IE)
    uapi_pin_set_ie(CONFIG_UART_RXD_PIN, PIN_IE_1);
#endif /* CONFIG_PINCTRL_SUPPORT_IE */
    uapi_pin_set_mode(CONFIG_UART_TXD_PIN, CONFIG_UART_TXD_PIN_MODE);
    uapi_pin_set_mode(CONFIG_UART_RXD_PIN, CONFIG_UART_RXD_PIN_MODE);
}

static void app_uart_init_config(void)
{
    uart_attr_t attr = {
        .baud_rate = UART_BAUDRATE,
        .data_bits = UART_DATA_BIT_8,
        .stop_bits = UART_STOP_BIT_1,
        .parity = UART_PARITY_NONE，
        .flow_ctrl = 0       /* Flow control. Setting it to 0 means flow control is disabled */
    };

    uart_pin_config_t pin_config = {
        .tx_pin = CONFIG_UART_TXD_PIN,
        .rx_pin = CONFIG_UART_RXD_PIN,
        .cts_pin = PIN_NONE,
        .rts_pin = PIN_NONE
    };

#if defined(CONFIG_UART_SUPPORT_DMA)
    uart_extra_attr_t extra_attr = {
        .tx_dma_enable = true,
        .tx_int_threshold = UART_FIFO_INT_TX_LEVEL_EQ_0_CHARACTER,
        .rx_dma_enable = true,
        .rx_int_threshold = UART_FIFO_INT_RX_LEVEL_1_CHARACTER
    };
    uapi_dma_init();
    uapi_dma_open();
    uapi_uart_deinit(CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID); /* The call here is only to exclude configurations used elsewhere to avoid interference. */
    uapi_uart_init(CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID, &pin_config, &attr, &extra_attr, &g_app_uart_buffer_config);
#else
    uapi_uart_deinit(CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID);
    uapi_uart_init(CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID, &pin_config, &attr, NULL, &g_app_uart_buffer_config);
#endif
}

#if defined(CONFIG_UART_SUPPORT_INT_MODE)
static void app_uart_read_int_handler(const void *buffer, uint16_t length, bool error)
{
    unused(error);
    if (buffer == NULL || length == 0) {
        osal_printk("uart%d int mode transfer illegal data!\r\n", CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID);
        return;
    }

    uint8_t *buff = (uint8_t *)buffer;
    if (memcpy_s(g_app_uart_rx_buff, length, buff, length) != EOK) {
        osal_printk("uart%d int mode data copy fail!\r\n", CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID);
        return;
    }
    if (memcpy_s(g_app_uart_int_rx_buff + g_app_uart_int_index, length, g_app_uart_rx_buff, length) != EOK) {
        g_app_uart_int_index = 0;
        osal_printk("uart%d int mode data2 copy fail!\r\n", CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID);
    }
    g_app_uart_int_index += length;
    g_app_uart_int_rx_flag = 1;
}

static void app_uart_write_int_handler(const void *buffer, uint32_t length, const void *params)
{
    unused(params);
    uint8_t *buff = (void *)buffer;
    for (uint8_t i = 0; i < length; i++) {
        osal_printk("uart%d write data[%d] = %d\r\n", CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID, i, buff[i]);
    }
}

static void app_uart_register_rx_callback(void)
{
    osal_printk("uart%d int mode register receive callback start!\r\n", CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID);
    if (uapi_uart_register_rx_callback(CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID, UART_RX_CONDITION_FULL_OR_SUFFICIENT_DATA_OR_IDLE,
                                       1, app_uart_read_int_handler) == ERRCODE_SUCC) {
        osal_printk("uart%d int mode register receive callback succ!\r\n", CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID);
    }
}
#endif

static void *uart_task(const char *arg)
{
    unused(arg);
#if defined(CONFIG_UART_SUPPORT_DMA)
    int32_t ret = CONFIG_UART_TRANSFER_SIZE;
#if defined(CONFIG_UART_USING_V151)
    ret = ERRCODE_SUCC;
#endif
#endif
    /* UART pinmux. */
    app_uart_init_pin();

    /* UART init config. */
    app_uart_init_config();

#if defined(CONFIG_UART_SUPPORT_INT_MODE)
    app_uart_register_rx_callback();
#endif

    while (1) {
#if defined(CONFIG_UART_SUPPORT_INT_MODE)
        while (g_app_uart_int_rx_flag != 1) { osal_msleep(CONFIG_UART_INT_WAIT_MS); }
        g_app_uart_int_rx_flag = 0;
        osal_printk("uart%d int mode send back!\r\n", CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID);
        if (uapi_uart_write_int(CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID, g_app_uart_int_rx_buff, CONFIG_UART_TRANSFER_SIZE, 0,
                                app_uart_write_int_handler) == ERRCODE_SUCC) {
            osal_printk("uart%d int mode send back succ!\r\n", CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID);
        }
#elif defined(CONFIG_UART_SUPPORT_DMA)
        osal_printk("uart%d dma mode receive start!\r\n", CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID);
        if (uapi_uart_read_by_dma(CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID, g_app_uart_rx_buff, CONFIG_UART_TRANSFER_SIZE,
            &g_app_dma_cfg) == ret) {
            osal_printk("uart%d dma mode receive succ!\r\n", CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID);
        }
        osal_printk("uart%d dma mode send back!\r\n", CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID);
        if (uapi_uart_write_by_dma(CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID, g_app_uart_rx_buff, CONFIG_UART_TRANSFER_SIZE,
            &g_app_dma_cfg) == ret) {
            osal_printk("uart%d dma mode send back succ!\r\n", CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID);
        }
#else
        osal_printk("uart%d poll mode receive start!\r\n", CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID);
        (void)uapi_watchdog_kick();
        if (uapi_uart_read(CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID, g_app_uart_rx_buff, CONFIG_UART_TRANSFER_SIZE,
            0) == CONFIG_UART_TRANSFER_SIZE) {
            osal_printk("uart%d poll mode receive succ!\r\n", CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID);
        }
        osal_printk("uart%d poll mode send back!\r\n", CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID);
        if (uapi_uart_write(CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID, g_app_uart_rx_buff, CONFIG_UART_TRANSFER_SIZE,
            0) == CONFIG_UART_TRANSFER_SIZE) {
            osal_printk("uart%d poll mode send back succ!\r\n", CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID);
        }
#endif
    }

    return NULL;
}

static void uart_entry(void)
{
    osal_task *task_handle = NULL;
    osal_kthread_lock();
    task_handle = osal_kthread_create((osal_kthread_handler)uart_task, 0, "UartTask", UART_TASK_STACK_SIZE);
    if (task_handle != NULL) {
        osal_kthread_set_priority(task_handle, UART_TASK_PRIO);
    }
    osal_kthread_unlock();
}

/* Run the uart_entry. */
app_run(uart_entry);

------------------------------------------------------------------

For the complete code of two-board UART DMA linked-list transmission and reception, see the following code:
Master-side code:
#include <string.h>
#include <stdlib.h>
#include <time.h>
#include "app_init.h"
#include "osal_task.h"
#include "dma.h"
#include "uart.h"
#include "osal_debug.h"
#define UART_TASK_PRIO                     24
#define UART_TASK_DURATION_MS              500
#define UART_TASK_STACK_SIZE               0x1000
#define UART_PAYLOAD_LEN                   255
rn uart_pin_config_t g_pin_config_map[UART_BUS_MAX_NUM];
static uint8_t g_app_uart_tx_payload_buff[UART_PAYLOAD_LEN] = { 0 };
static uint8_t g_app_uart_rx_payload_buff[UART_PAYLOAD_LEN] = { 0 };
volatile uint32_t g_success = 0;
volatile uint32_t g_fail = 0;
static void uart_dma_lli_write_cb(uint8_t channel, errcode_t result)
{
    unused(result);
    unused(channel);
}
static bool uart_dma_lli_read_cb(uint8_t channel, uint16_t length, errcode_t result)
{
    unused(channel);
    unused(length);
    unused(result);
    if (memcmp(g_app_uart_rx_payload_buff, g_app_uart_tx_payload_buff, UART_PAYLOAD_LEN) == 0) {
        g_success++;
    } else {
        g_fail++;
        for (uint32_t i = 0; i < UART_PAYLOAD_LEN; i++) {
            osal_printk("[%d] tx:0x%x,rx:0x%x\r\n", i, g_app_uart_tx_payload_buff[i], g_app_uart_rx_payload_buff[i]);
        }
    }
    if ((g_success + g_fail) % 0x400 == 0) {
        osal_printk("g_success:%d\r\n", g_success);
        osal_printk("g_fail:%d\r\n", g_fail);
    }
    for (uint16_t i = 0; i < UART_PAYLOAD_LEN; i++) {
        g_app_uart_tx_payload_buff[i] = rand() % 0xff;
    }
    uapi_uart_dma_send(CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID, g_app_uart_tx_payload_buff, UART_PAYLOAD_LEN, uart_dma_lli_write_cb);
    return true;
}
static void uart_dma_lli_master_entry(void)
{
    for (uint16_t i = 0; i < UART_PAYLOAD_LEN; i++) {
        g_app_uart_tx_payload_buff[i] = rand() % 0xff;
    }
    uapi_pin_set_pull(g_pin_config_map[bus].rx_pin, PIN_PULL_UP);
    uart_port_lli_init_config(CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID);
    uapi_uart_dma_recv_register(CONFIG_UABUS_ID, g_app_uart_rx_payload_buff, UART_PAYLOAD_LEN, uart_dma_lli_read_cb);
    uapi_uart_dma_send(CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID, g_app_uart_tx_payload_buff, UART_PAYLOAD_LEN, uart_dma_lli_write_cb);
    return;
}
/* Run the uart_dma_lli_master_entry. */
app_run(uart_dma_lli_master_entry);

#include "app_init.h"
#include "uart.h"
#include "osal_debug.h"
#define UART_TASK_PRIO                     24
#define UART_TASK_DURATION_MS              500
#define UART_TASK_STACK_SIZE               0x1000
#define UART_PAYLOAD_LEN                   255
extern uart_pin_config_t g_pin_config_map[UART_BUS_MAX_NUM];
static uint8_t g_app_uart_tx_payload_buff[UART_PAYLOAD_LEN] = { 0 };
static uint8_t g_app_uart_rx_payload_buff[UART_PAYLOAD_LEN] = { 0 };
static uint8_t g_app_uart_rx_payload_buff1[UART_PAYLOAD_LEN] = { 0 };
static bool g_double_rx_buffer = true;
volatile uint32_t g_recv_count = 0;
static void uart_dma_lli_write_cb(uint8_t channel, errcode_t result)
{
    unused(result & channel);
}
static bool uart_dma_lli_read_cb(uint8_t channel, uint16_t length, errcode_t result)
{
    unused(channel);
    unused(length);
    unused(result);
    if (g_recv_count++ % 0x200 == 0) {
        osal_printk("g_recv_count:%d\r\n", g_recv_count);
    }
    if (g_double_rx_buffer) {
        uapi_uart_dma_send(CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID, g_app_uart_rx_payload_buff, UART_PAYLOAD_LEN, uart_dma_lli_write_cb);
        uapi_uart_update_dma_recv_buff(CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID, g_app_uart_rx_payload_buff1, UART_PAYLOAD_LEN);
        g_double_rx_buffer = false;
    } else {
        uapi_uart_dma_send(CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID, g_app_uart_rx_payload_buff1, UART_PAYLOAD_LEN, uart_dma_lli_write_cb);
        uapi_uart_update_dma_recv_buff(CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID, g_app_uart_rx_payload_buff, UART_PAYLOAD_LEN);
        g_double_rx_buffer = true;
    }
    return true;
}
static void uart_dma_lli_slave_entry(void)
{
    uapi_pin_set_pull(g_pin_config_map[bus].rx_pin, PIN_PULL_UP);
    uart_port_lli_init_config(CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID);
    uapi_uart_dma_recv_register(CONFIG_UART_BUS_ID, g_app_uart_rx_payload_buff, UART_PAYLOAD_LEN,
                                uart_dma_lli_read_cb);
    return;
}
/* Run the uart_dma_lli_slave_entry. */
app_run(uart_dma_lli_slave_entry);
```

## Precautions<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001664983946"></a>

-   In the SDK, UART0 (UART\_L0) is used by default as the shared serial port for program burning, Testsuite, AT, and data printing.
-   In the SDK, UART1 (UART\_H0) is used by default as the data channel for DebugKits maintenance and testing.
-   When DMA transmission is used, the RX pin of UART must be configured as input pull-up during initialization.
-   The UART pin configuration takes effect in the global variable g\_pin\_config\_map in drivers/chips/bs2x/porting/uart/uart\_porting.c.

# SPI<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713023517"></a>






## Overview<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001664983958"></a>

SPI (Serial Peripheral Interface) is a high-speed, full-duplex, synchronous communication bus. It enables the MCU to communicate with various peripheral devices in a serial manner to exchange information. The SPI bus can be directly connected to a variety of standard peripheral devices produced by various manufacturers, including FLASH, RAM, network controllers, LCD display drivers, A/D converters, and MCUs. A standard SPI bus generally uses four lines: the serial clock line (SCLK), the master input/slave output data line MISO, the master output/slave input data line MOSI, and the active-low slave select line CS.

This product provides three groups of configurable full-duplex standard SPI peripherals, SPI0 to SPI2. The SPI specifications are as follows:

-   All three groups support Master/Slave mode.
-   Each SPI has separate 32-bit x 8 FIFOs for transmission and reception.
-   The maximum transmission bit width of 32 bits is supported.
-   All three groups support single-line (standard) and three-line modes.

## Function Description<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001664983954"></a>

>![](public_sys-resources/icon-note.gif) **Note:** 
>If the SPI driver needs to configure DMA mode to read/write data, ensure that the DMA driver has been initialized. For DMA initialization, refer to “[DMA](DMA.md)”.

SPI master mode supports read/write in polling, interrupt, and DMA modes; slave mode supports read/write in interrupt and DMA modes.

BS2X SPI supports single-line SPI and three-line SPI:

-   Single-line SPI, namely the standard SPI, has four pins: CLK, CS, MISO, and MOSI. Standard SPI is full-duplex, where MISO and MOSI transmit data in different directions, and data transmission and reception can proceed simultaneously. The default configuration in the library is standard SPI, and no modification is required except for the pin configuration.
-   Three-line SPI has three pins: CLK, CS, and MOSI. In this case, SPI is half-duplex, with only one line for data transmission and reception. The master can only send or receive slave data, and transmission and reception cannot proceed simultaneously.

The interfaces and functions provided by the SPI module are as follows:

-   uapi\_spi\_init（spi\_bus\_t bus, spi\_attr\_t \*attr, spi\_extra\_attr\_t \*extra\_attr）：Initialize SPI (including settings such as master/slave device, polarity, phase, frame protocol, transmission frequency, and transmission bit width)（bus indicates the SPI bus in use，attr indicates the basic configuration parameters of SPI，extra\_attr indicates the advanced configuration parameters of SPI）。
-   uapi\_spi\_deinit（spi\_bus\_t bus）：Deinitialize SPI (close the corresponding SPI unit and release resources)（bus indicates the SPI bus in use）。
-   uapi\_spi\_set\_tmod（spi\_bus\_t bus, hal\_spi\_trans\_mode\_t tmod, uint8\_t data\_frame\_num）：Set the SPI transmission mode（bus indicates the SPI bus in use，tmod indicates the SPI transmission mode，data\_frame\_num indicates the number of SPI RX data frames）。
-   uapi\_spi\_set\_attr（spi\_bus\_t bus, spi\_attr\_t \*attr）：Set the basic configuration parameters of SPI（bus indicates the SPI bus in use，attr indicates the basic configuration parameters of SPI）。
-   uapi\_spi\_get\_attr（spi\_bus\_t bus, spi\_attr\_t \*attr）:  Get the basic configuration parameters of SPI (master/slave mode, clock polarity, clock phase, clock divider coefficient, SPI working frequency, serial transmission protocol, SPI frame format, SPI frame length, SPI transmission mode, etc.)（bus indicates the SPI bus in use，attr indicates the basic configuration parameters of SPI）。
-   uapi\_spi\_set\_extra\_attr（spi\_bus\_t bus, spi\_extra\_attr\_t \*extra\_attr）：Set the advanced configuration parameters of SPI（bus indicates the SPI bus in use，attr indicates the advanced configuration parameters of SPI）。
-   uapi\_spi\_get\_extra\_attr（spi\_bus\_t bus, spi\_extra\_attr\_t \*extra\_attr）：Get the advanced configuration parameters of SPI (whether SPI uses DMA for data sending and reception, QSPI parameters, etc.)（bus indicates the SPI bus in use，attr indicates the advanced configuration parameters of SPI）。
-   uapi\_spi\_set\_dma\_mode（spi\_bus\_t bus, bool en, const spi\_dma\_config\_t \*dma\_cfg）：Enable/disable SPI transmission in DMA mode（bus indicates the SPI bus in use，en indicates whether to enable DMA transmission，dma\_cfg indicates the DMA configuration of SPI）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_SPI\_SUPPORT\_DMA macro is enabled and the CONFIG\_SPI\_SUPPORT\_POLL\_AND\_DMA\_AUTO\_SWITCH macro is disabled.)
-   uapi\_spi\_set\_irq\_mode（spi\_bus\_t bus, bool irq\_en, spi\_rx\_callback\_t rx\_callback, spi\_tx\_callback\_t tx\_callback）：Enable/disable SPI data transmission in interrupt mode on the master（bus indicates the SPI bus in use，irq\_en indicates whether to enable interrupt transmission，rx\_callback indicates the callback function called when data reception is complete，tx\_callback indicates the callback function called when data sending is complete）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_SPI\_SUPPORT\_INTERRUPT macro is enabled and the CONFIG\_SPI\_SUPPORT\_POLL\_AND\_DMA\_AUTO\_SWITCH macro is disabled.)
-   uapi\_spi\_set\_loop\_back\_mode（spi\_bus\_t bus, bool loopback\_en）：Set the loopback test mode（bus indicates the SPI bus in use，loopback\_en indicates whether to enable loopback mode transmission）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_SPI\_SUPPORT\_LOOPBACK macro is enabled. It is not implemented in this product.)
-   uapi\_spi\_set\_crc\_mode（spi\_bus\_t bus, const spi\_crc\_config\_t \*crc\_config, spi\_crc\_err\_callback\_t cb）：Set the SPI transmission and reception CRC mode（bus indicates the SPI bus in use，crc\_config indicates the CRC parameters of SPI，cb indicates the CRC check error callback function）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_SPI\_SUPPORT\_CRC macro is enabled. It is not implemented in this product.)
-   uapi\_spi\_select\_slave（spi\_bus\_t bus, spi\_slave\_t cs）：Select the slave device to communicate with in master mode（bus indicates the SPI bus in use，cs indicates the selected slave device）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_SPI\_SUPPORT\_MASTER macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_spi\_master\_write（spi\_bus\_t bus, const spi\_xfer\_data\_t \*data, uint32\_t timeout）：The SPI master sends data to the slave（bus indicates the SPI bus in use，data indicates the data transmitted by SPI，timeout indicates the timeout period of the current transmission）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_SPI\_SUPPORT\_MASTER macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_spi\_master\_read（spi\_bus\_t bus, const spi\_xfer\_data\_t \*data, uint32\_t timeout）：The SPI master reads data from the slave（bus indicates the SPI bus in use，data indicates the data transmitted by SPI，timeout indicates the timeout period of the current transmission）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_SPI\_SUPPORT\_MASTER macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_spi\_master\_writeread（spi\_bus\_t bus, const spi\_xfer\_data\_t \*data, uint32\_t timeout）：The SPI master transmits and receives data in full-duplex mode（bus indicates the SPI bus in use，data indicates the data transmitted by SPI，timeout indicates the timeout period of the current transmission）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_SPI\_SUPPORT\_MASTER macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_spi\_slave\_write（spi\_bus\_t bus, const spi\_xfer\_data\_t \*data, uint32\_t timeout）：The SPI slave sends data to the master（bus indicates the SPI bus in use，data indicates the data transmitted by SPI，timeout indicates the timeout period of the current transmission）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_SPI\_SUPPORT\_SLAVE macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_spi\_slave\_read（spi\_bus\_t bus, const spi\_xfer\_data\_t \*data, uint32\_t timeout）：The SPI slave reads data from the master（bus indicates the SPI bus in use，data indicates the data transmitted by SPI，timeout indicates the timeout period of the current transmission）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_SPI\_SUPPORT\_SLAVE macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_spi\_slave\_writeread（spi\_bus\_t bus, const spi\_xfer\_data\_t \*data, uint32\_t timeout）：The SPI slave transmits and receives data in full-duplex mode（bus indicates the SPI bus in use，data indicates the data transmitted by SPI，timeout indicates the timeout period of the current transmission）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_SPI\_SUPPORT\_SLAVE macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_spi\_suspend（uintptr\_t arg）：Suspend all SPI channels for low-power scenarios（arg indicates the parameters required for suspension）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_SPI\_SUPPORT\_LPM macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_spi\_resume（uintptr\_t arg）：Resume all SPI channels for low-power scenarios（arg indicates the parameters required for resume）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_SPI\_SUPPORT\_LPM macro is enabled.)
-   spi\_porting\_set\_rx\_mode\(spi\_bus\_t bus, uint16\_t num\)：Configure SPI to receive data in EEPROM mode. num indicates how many data units form one transmission.
-   spi\_porting\_set\_tx\_mode\(spi\_bus\_t bus\)：Configure SPI to send data in TX mode.
-   spi\_porting\_set\_txrx\_mode\(spi\_bus\_t bus\)：Configure SPI to transmit and receive data in TXRX mode.

## KCONFIG Configuration<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001896207234"></a>

The Kconfig macro configuration of single-line (standard) SPI is as follows:

![](figures/zh-cn_image_0000002039193673.png)

![](figures/zh-cn_image_0000002150654017.png)

>![](public_sys-resources/icon-note.gif) **Note:** 
>If the images above are inconsistent with the table descriptions, the table prevails.

The specific descriptions of the configuration macros are shown in [Table 1](#table16629938173916).

**Table 1**  Descriptions of macros related to SPI

<a name="table16629938173916"></a>

<tbody><tr id="row16629338103916"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="22.62773722627737%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.1 "><p id="p166291338133910"><a name="p166291338133910"></a><a name="p166291338133910"></a>CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_MASTER</p>

</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="19.938006199380062%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.3 "><p id="p182789183235"><a name="p182789183235"></a><a name="p182789183235"></a>SPI supports master selection. Enable it as appropriate.</p>
</td>

</td>

</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="19.938006199380062%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.3 "><p id="p750495852813"><a name="p750495852813"></a><a name="p750495852813"></a>SPI supports slave selection. Enable it as appropriate.</p>
</td>

</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="11.348865113488651%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.5 "><p id="p2629138193917"><a name="p2629138193917"></a><a name="p2629138193917"></a>No</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="14.3985601439856%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.6 "><p id="p16717115134416"><a name="p16717115134416"></a><a name="p16717115134416"></a>Enabled by default. Recommended to enable.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row3629193823919"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="22.62773722627737%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.1 "><p id="p262983811395"><a name="p262983811395"></a><a name="p262983811395"></a>CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_DMA</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="21.07789221077892%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.2 "><p id="p53508282388"><a name="p53508282388"></a><a name="p53508282388"></a>SPI support DMA transfer.</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="19.938006199380062%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.3 "><p id="p1164428144915"><a name="p1164428144915"></a><a name="p1164428144915"></a>SPI supports the DMA mode. Enable it as appropriate. If the SPI DMA mode is selected, the KCONFIG configuration of the DMA driver must be selected as well. For details, refer to "<a href="KCONFIG配置-27.md">KCONFIG Configuration</a>".</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="10.608939106089391%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.4 "><p id="p3629133873917"><a name="p3629133873917"></a><a name="p3629133873917"></a>Yes</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="11.348865113488651%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.5 "><p id="p8473437181816"><a name="p8473437181816"></a><a name="p8473437181816"></a>CONFIG_DRIVER_SUPPORT_DMA</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="14.3985601439856%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.6 "><p id="p732198195217"><a name="p732198195217"></a><a name="p732198195217"></a>Disabled by default. Enable it as appropriate.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row962920381394"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="22.62773722627737%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.1 "><p id="p1629738173912"><a name="p1629738173912"></a><a name="p1629738173912"></a>CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_POLL_AND_DMA_AUTO_SWITCH</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="21.07789221077892%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.2 "><p id="p1326114416384"><a name="p1326114416384"></a><a name="p1326114416384"></a>SPI support poll and dma mode switch.</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="19.938006199380062%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.3 "><p id="p7601132985220"><a name="p7601132985220"></a><a name="p7601132985220"></a>SPI supports automatically switching between polling and DMA modes based on the set threshold. Enable it as appropriate.</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="10.608939106089391%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.4 "><p id="p7630938143910"><a name="p7630938143910"></a><a name="p7630938143910"></a>Yes</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="11.348865113488651%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.5 "><p id="p184291153201819"><a name="p184291153201819"></a><a name="p184291153201819"></a>CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_DMA</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="14.3985601439856%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.6 "><p id="p55269547522"><a name="p55269547522"></a><a name="p55269547522"></a>Disabled by default. Enable it as appropriate.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row62681237271"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="22.62773722627737%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.1 "><p id="p112401826112812"><a name="p112401826112812"></a><a name="p112401826112812"></a>CONFIG_SPI_AUTO_SWITCH_DMA_THRESHOLD</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="21.07789221077892%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.2 "><p id="p1844945715385"><a name="p1844945715385"></a><a name="p1844945715385"></a>SPI indicates the threshold of the automatic switchover mode.</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="19.938006199380062%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.3 "><p id="p1369200125313"><a name="p1369200125313"></a><a name="p1369200125313"></a>The threshold set for automatically switching between polling and DMA modes. The default value is 32.</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="10.608939106089391%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.4 "><p id="p5269202314277"><a name="p5269202314277"></a><a name="p5269202314277"></a>Yes</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="11.348865113488651%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.5 "><p id="p115505376560"><a name="p115505376560"></a><a name="p115505376560"></a>CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_POLL_AND_DMA_AUTO_SWITCH</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="14.3985601439856%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.6 "><p id="p91685144576"><a name="p91685144576"></a><a name="p91685144576"></a>The default value is 32. Configure it as appropriate.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row17817572040"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="22.62773722627737%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.1 "><p id="p109831846175713"><a name="p109831846175713"></a><a name="p109831846175713"></a>CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_INTERRUPT</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="21.07789221077892%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.2 "><p id="p124319334619"><a name="p124319334619"></a><a name="p124319334619"></a>SPI support interrupt transfer.</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="19.938006199380062%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.3 "><p id="p0369111616584"><a name="p0369111616584"></a><a name="p0369111616584"></a>SPI supports the interrupt mode. Enable it as appropriate.</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="10.608939106089391%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.4 "><p id="p17919571246"><a name="p17919571246"></a><a name="p17919571246"></a>Yes</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="11.348865113488651%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.5 "><p id="p12620160105914"><a name="p12620160105914"></a><a name="p12620160105914"></a>No</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="14.3985601439856%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.6 "><p id="p182001731221"><a name="p182001731221"></a><a name="p182001731221"></a>Disabled by default. Enable it as appropriate.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row1071318918131"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="22.62773722627737%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.1 "><p id="p671319910134"><a name="p671319910134"></a><a name="p671319910134"></a>CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_CONCURRENCY</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="21.07789221077892%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.2 "><p id="p438114181463"><a name="p438114181463"></a><a name="p438114181463"></a>SPI support concurrency and the mode can be switched only after the transmission is complete.</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="19.938006199380062%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.3 "><p id="p17131797138"><a name="p17131797138"></a><a name="p17131797138"></a>If this macro is enabled, the integrity of the current transmission can be ensured. The next transmission starts only after the current transmission is complete. This macro must be enabled when no lock is added.</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="10.608939106089391%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.4 "><p id="p1571316971311"><a name="p1571316971311"></a><a name="p1571316971311"></a>Yes</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="11.348865113488651%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.5 "><p id="p37135917132"><a name="p37135917132"></a><a name="p37135917132"></a>No</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="14.3985601439856%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.6 "><p id="p57965521033"><a name="p57965521033"></a><a name="p57965521033"></a>Disabled by default. Enable it as appropriate.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row1663153711314"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="22.62773722627737%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.1 "><p id="p13663183721310"><a name="p13663183721310"></a><a name="p13663183721310"></a>CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_LOOPBACK</p>
</td>

</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="19.938006199380062%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.3 "><p id="p185305517595"><a name="p185305517595"></a><a name="p185305517595"></a>SPI supports the loopback mode, which is not implemented in this product.</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="10.608939106089391%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.4 "><p id="p186635371131"><a name="p186635371131"></a><a name="p186635371131"></a>No</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="11.348865113488651%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.5 "><p id="p1366323711131"><a name="p1366323711131"></a><a name="p1366323711131"></a>No</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="14.3985601439856%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.6 "><p id="p5326122414510"><a name="p5326122414510"></a><a name="p5326122414510"></a>Disabled by default, but not yet implemented in this product; it is recommended to disable it.</p>
</td>
</tr>

</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="21.07789221077892%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.2 "><p id="p1211594421319"><a name="p1211594421319"></a><a name="p1211594421319"></a>SPI support CRC.</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="19.938006199380062%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.3 "><p id="p4115184491311"><a name="p4115184491311"></a><a name="p4115184491311"></a>SPI supports the CRC mode, which is not implemented in this product.</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="10.608939106089391%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.4 "><p id="p9115124411312"><a name="p9115124411312"></a><a name="p9115124411312"></a>No</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="11.348865113488651%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.5 "><p id="p5115544151311"><a name="p5115544151311"></a><a name="p5115544151311"></a>No</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="14.3985601439856%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.6 "><p id="p1518312251351"><a name="p1518312251351"></a><a name="p1518312251351"></a>Disabled by default, but not yet implemented in this product; it is recommended to disable it.</p>
</td>
</tr>
