## Development Guide<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001664983974"></a>

SPI is used to connect devices that support the SPI protocol. The SPI unit can act as a master or slave device. The following uses the SPI unit as a master device as an example. The write operation is as follows:

1.  Use IO mux to set the pins used by the SPI function to the SPI function.

    For the pin mux functions, refer to the definitions of each pin function in platform\_core.h.

    ```
    #define SPI_PIN_MISO_PINMUX     HAL_PIO_SPI2_RXD
    #define SPI_PIN_MOSI_PINMUX     HAL_PIO_SPI2_TXD
    #define SPI_PIN_CLK_PINMUX      HAL_PIO_SPI2_CLK
    #define SPI_PIN_CS_PINMUX       HAL_PIO_SPI2_CS0
    #define SPI_PIN_MISO    S_MGPIO16
    #define SPI_PIN_MOSI    S_MGPIO17
    #define SPI_PIN_CLK     S_MGPIO18
    #define SPI_PIN_CS      S_MGPIO19
    void usr_spi_io_init(void)
    {
        /* Set the SPI pinmux */
        uapi_pin_set_mode(SPI_PIN_MISO, SPI_PIN_MISO_PINMUX);       /* Set the SPI MISO pinmux */
        uapi_pin_set_mode(SPI_PIN_MOSI, SPI_PIN_MOSI_PINMUX);       /* Set the SPI MOSI pinmux */
        uapi_pin_set_mode(SPI_PIN_CLK, SPI_PIN_CLK_PINMUX);         /* Set the SPI CLK pinmux */
        uapi_pin_set_mode(SPI_PIN_CS, SPI_PIN_CS_PINMUX);           /* Set the SPI CS pinmux */
    }
    ```

2.  Call uapi\_spi\_init to initialize SPI resources, select the SPI function unit, and configure SPI parameters.

    ```
    #define TEST_SPI                SPI_BUS_2
    #define BUS_CLOCK               32000000    /* 32M */
    #define SPI_FREQUENCY           2
    errcode_t usr_spi_init(void)
    {
        spi_attr_t config = { 0 };
        spi_extra_attr_t ext_config = { 0 };
        ext_config.sspi_param.wait_cycles = 0x10;
        usr_spi_io_init();
        config.freq_mhz = SPI_FREQUENCY;                            /* Working frequency after SPI frequency division */
        config.is_slave = false;                                    /* Master mode */
        config.frame_size = HAL_SPI_FRAME_SIZE_8;                   /* SPI frame size, 8 bits */
        config.salve_num = 1;                                       /* Use chip select 0 */
        config.spi_frame_format = HAL_SPI_FRAME_FORMAT_STANDARD;    /* SPI transmission mode: standard SPI */
        config.bus_clk = BUS_CLOCK;                                 /* SPI clock source frequency */
        config.frame_format = SPI_CFG_FRAME_FORMAT_MOTOROLA_SPI;    /* SPI protocol format: Motorola SPI protocol format */
        config.tmod = HAL_SPI_TRANS_MODE_TXRX;                      /* SPI transmission mode: transmit/receive mode */
        config.clk_phase = SPI_CFG_CLK_CPHA_0;                      /* SPI phase: SPI_CFG_CLK_CPHA_0 */
        config.clk_polarity = SPI_CFG_CLK_CPOL_0;                   /* SPI polarity: SPI_CFG_CLK_CPOL_0 */
        /* Initialize SPI */
        errcode_t err = uapi_spi_init(TEST_SPI, &config, &ext_config);
        return err;
    }
    ```

3.  Call uapi\_spi\_master\_writeread to perform the SPI master write/read operation.

    The following uses the master write/read as an example:

    ```
    errcode_t usr_spi_writeread(uint8_t *wdata, uint8_t wlen, uint8_t *rdata, uint8_t rlen)
    {
        spi_xfer_data_t spi_recv_xfer = { 0 };
        spi_recv_xfer.tx_buff = wdata;                               /* Set the TX buffer */
        spi_recv_xfer.tx_bytes = wlen;                               /* Set the TX buffer length */
        spi_recv_xfer.rx_buff = rdata;                               /* Set the RX buffer */
        spi_recv_xfer.rx_bytes = rlen;                               /* Set the RX buffer length */
        spi_porting_set_rx_mode(TEST_SPI, rlen);                     /* Set the RX reception mode of the write/read interface */
        return uapi_spi_master_writeread(TEST_SPI, &spi_recv_xfer, 100); /* Read data */
    }
    ```

For the complete master/slave transmission and reception code of two-board SPI in three modes (polling, interrupt, and DMA), see the following code:

```
#include "pinctrl.h"
#include "spi.h"
#include "soc_osal.h"
#include "app_init.h"
#include "dma.h"

#define SPI_SLAVE_NUM                   1
#define SPI_FREQUENCY                   2
#define SPI_CLK_POLARITY                0
#define SPI_CLK_PHASE                   0
#define SPI_FRAME_FORMAT                0
#define SPI_FRAME_FORMAT_STANDARD       0
#define SPI_FRAME_SIZE                  0x1f
#define SPI_TMOD                        0
#define SPI_WAIT_CYCLES                 0x10
#if defined(CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_DMA) && !(defined(CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_POLL_AND_DMA_AUTO_SWITCH))
#define SPI_DMA_WIDTH                   2
#endif
#if defined(CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_SUPPORT_QSPI)
#define QSPI_WRITE_CMD                  0x38
#define QSPI_WRITE_ADDR                 0x123
#endif
#define SPI_TASK_DURATION_MS            500
#define SPI_TASK_PRIO                   24
#define SPI_TASK_STACK_SIZE             0x1000

static void app_spi_init_pin(void)
{
#if defined(CONFIG_PINCTRL_SUPPORT_IE)
    uapi_pin_set_ie(CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_DI_PIN, PIN_IE_1);
#endif /* CONFIG_PINCTRL_SUPPORT_IE */
    uapi_pin_set_mode(CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_CLK_PIN, CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_CLK_PIN_MODE);
    uapi_pin_set_mode(CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_CS_PIN, CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_CS_PIN_MODE);
    uapi_pin_set_mode(CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_DI_PIN, CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_DI_PIN_MODE);
    uapi_pin_set_mode(CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_DO_PIN, CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_DO_PIN_MODE);
}

#if defined(CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_INTERRUPT) && (CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_INTERRUPT == 1)
static void app_spi_master_write_int_handler(const void *buffer, uint32_t length)
{
    unused(buffer);
    unused(length);
    osal_printk("spi master write interrupt start!\r\n");
}

static void app_spi_master_rx_callback(const void *buffer, uint32_t length, bool error)
{
    if (buffer == NULL || length == 0) {
        osal_printk("spi master transfer illegal data!\r\n");
        return;
    }
    if (error) {
        osal_printk("app_spi_master_read_int error!\r\n");
        return;
    }

    uint8_t *buff = (uint8_t *)buffer;
    for (uint32_t i = 0; i < length; i++) {
        osal_printk("buff[%d] = %x\r\n", i, buff[i]);
    }
    osal_printk("app_spi_master_read_int success!\r\n");
}
#endif  /* CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_INTERRUPT */

static void app_spi_master_init_config(void)
{
    spi_attr_t config = { 0 };
    spi_extra_attr_t ext_config = { 0 };

    config.is_slave = false;
    config.slave_num = SPI_SLAVE_NUM;
    config.bus_clk = SPI_CLK_FREQ;
    config.freq_mhz = SPI_FREQUENCY;
    config.clk_polarity = SPI_CLK_POLARITY;
    config.clk_phase = SPI_CLK_PHASE;
    config.frame_format = SPI_FRAME_FORMAT;
    config.spi_frame_format = HAL_SPI_FRAME_FORMAT_STANDARD;
    config.frame_size = SPI_FRAME_SIZE;
    config.tmod = SPI_TMOD;
    config.sste = 0;

    ext_config.qspi_param.wait_cycles = SPI_WAIT_CYCLES;
    uapi_spi_init(CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_BUS_ID, &config, &ext_config);
#if defined(CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_DMA) && (CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_DMA == 1)
    uapi_dma_init();
    uapi_dma_open();
#ifndef CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_POLL_AND_DMA_AUTO_SWITCH
    spi_dma_config_t dma_cfg = {
        .src_width = SPI_DMA_WIDTH,
        .dest_width = SPI_DMA_WIDTH,
        .burst_length = 0,
        .priority = 0
    };
    if (uapi_spi_set_dma_mode(CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_BUS_ID, true, &dma_cfg) != ERRCODE_SUCC) {
        osal_printk("spi%d master set dma mode fail!\r\n");
    }
#endif
#endif  /* CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_DMA */

#if defined(CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_INTERRUPT) && (CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_INTERRUPT == 1)
    if (uapi_spi_set_irq_mode(CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_BUS_ID, true, app_spi_master_rx_callback,
        app_spi_master_write_int_handler) == ERRCODE_SUCC) {
        osal_printk("spi%d master set irq mode succ!\r\n", CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_BUS_ID);
    }
#endif  /* CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_INTERRUPT */
}

static void *spi_master_task(const char *arg)
{
    unused(arg);
    /* SPI pinmux. */
    app_spi_init_pin();

    /* SPI master init config. */
    app_spi_master_init_config();

    /* SPI data config. */
    uint8_t tx_data[CONFIG_SPI_TRANSFER_LEN] = { 0 };
    for (uint32_t loop = 0; loop < CONFIG_SPI_TRANSFER_LEN; loop++) {
        tx_data[loop] = (loop & 0xFF);
    }
    uint8_t rx_data[CONFIG_SPI_TRANSFER_LEN] = { 0 };
    spi_xfer_data_t data = {
        .tx_buff = tx_data,
        .tx_bytes = CONFIG_SPI_TRANSFER_LEN,
        .rx_buff = rx_data,
        .rx_bytes = CONFIG_SPI_TRANSFER_LEN,
#if defined(CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_SUPPORT_QSPI)
        .cmd = QSPI_WRITE_CMD,
        .addr = QSPI_WRITE_ADDR,
#endif
    };

    while (1) {
        osal_msleep(SPI_TASK_DURATION_MS);
#ifndef CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_SUPPORT_WRITEREAD
        osal_printk("spi%d master send start!\r\n", CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_BUS_ID);
        if (uapi_spi_master_write(CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_BUS_ID, &data, 0xFFFFFFFF) == ERRCODE_SUCC) {
            osal_printk("spi%d master send succ!\r\n", CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_BUS_ID);
        } else {
            continue;
        }
        osal_printk("spi%d master receive start!\r\n", CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_BUS_ID);
        if (uapi_spi_master_read(CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_BUS_ID, &data, 0xFFFFFFFF) == ERRCODE_SUCC) {
#ifndef CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_INTERRUPT
            for (uint32_t i = 0; i < data.rx_bytes; i++) {
                osal_printk("spi%d master receive data is %x\r\n", CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_BUS_ID, data.rx_buff[i]);
            }
#endif
            osal_printk("spi%d master receive succ!\r\n", CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_BUS_ID);
        }
#else
        osal_printk("spi%d master writeread start!\r\n", CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_BUS_ID);
        if (uapi_spi_master_writeread(CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_BUS_ID, &data, 0xFFFFFFFF) == ERRCODE_SUCC) {
            for (uint32_t i = 0; i < data.rx_bytes; i++) {
                osal_printk("spi%d master writeread data is %x\r\n", CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_BUS_ID, data.rx_buff[i]);
            }
            osal_printk("spi%d master writeread succ!\r\n", CONFIG_SPI_MASTER_BUS_ID);
        }
#endif
    }

    return NULL;
}

static void spi_master_entry(void)
{
    osal_task *task_handle = NULL;
    osal_kthread_lock();
    task_handle = osal_kthread_create((osal_kthread_handler)spi_master_task, 0, "SpiMasterTask", SPI_TASK_STACK_SIZE);
    if (task_handle != NULL) {
        osal_kthread_set_priority(task_handle, SPI_TASK_PRIO);
    }
    osal_kthread_unlock();
}

/* Run the spi_master_entry. */
app_run(spi_master_entry);

------------------------------------------------------------------------------

#include "pinctrl.h"
#include "spi.h"
#include "soc_osal.h"
#include "app_init.h"
#include "dma.h"

#define SPI_SLAVE_NUM                   1
#define SPI_FREQUENCY                   2
#define SPI_CLK_POLARITY                0
#define SPI_CLK_PHASE                   0
#define SPI_FRAME_FORMAT                0
#define SPI_FRAME_FORMAT_STANDARD       0
#define SPI_FRAME_SIZE                  0x1f
#define SPI_TMOD                        0
#define SPI_WAIT_CYCLES                 0x10
#if defined(CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_DMA) && !(defined(CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_POLL_AND_DMA_AUTO_SWITCH))
#define SPI_DMA_WIDTH                   2
#endif

#define SPI_TASK_DURATION_MS            500
#define SPI_TASK_PRIO                   24
#define SPI_TASK_STACK_SIZE             0x1000

static void app_spi_init_pin(void)
{
#if defined(CONFIG_PINCTRL_SUPPORT_IE)
    uapi_pin_set_ie(CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_CLK_PIN, PIN_IE_1);
    uapi_pin_set_ie(CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_CS_PIN, PIN_IE_1);
    uapi_pin_set_ie(CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_DI_PIN, PIN_IE_1);
#endif /* CONFIG_PINCTRL_SUPPORT_IE */
    uapi_pin_set_mode(CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_CLK_PIN, CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_CLK_PIN_MODE);
    uapi_pin_set_mode(CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_CS_PIN, CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_CS_PIN_MODE);
    uapi_pin_set_mode(CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_DI_PIN, CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_DI_PIN_MODE);
    uapi_pin_set_mode(CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_DO_PIN, CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_DO_PIN_MODE);
}

#if defined(CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_INTERRUPT) && (CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_INTERRUPT == 1)
static void app_spi_slave_write_int_handler(const void *buffer, uint32_t length)
{
    unused(buffer);
    unused(length);
    osal_printk("spi slave write interrupt start!\r\n");
}

static void app_spi_slave_rx_callback(const void *buffer, uint32_t length, bool error)
{
    if (buffer == NULL || length == 0) {
        osal_printk("spi slave transfer illegal data!\r\n");
        return;
    }
    if (error) {
        osal_printk("app_spi_slave_read_int error!\r\n");
        return;
    }

    uint8_t *buff = (uint8_t *)buffer;
    for (uint32_t i = 0; i < length; i++) {
        osal_printk("buff[%d] = %x\r\n", i, buff[i]);
    }
    osal_printk("app_spi_slave_read_int success!\r\n");
}
#endif  /* CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_INTERRUPT */

static void app_spi_slave_init_config(void)
{
    spi_attr_t config = { 0 };
    spi_extra_attr_t ext_config = { 0 };

    config.is_slave = true;
    config.slave_num = SPI_SLAVE_NUM;
    config.bus_clk = SPI_CLK_FREQ;
    config.freq_mhz = SPI_FREQUENCY;
    config.clk_polarity = SPI_CLK_POLARITY;
    config.clk_phase = SPI_CLK_PHASE;
    config.frame_format = SPI_FRAME_FORMAT;
    config.spi_frame_format = HAL_SPI_FRAME_FORMAT_STANDARD;
    config.frame_size = SPI_FRAME_SIZE;
    config.tmod = SPI_TMOD;
    config.sste = 0;

    ext_config.qspi_param.wait_cycles = SPI_WAIT_CYCLES;

    uapi_spi_init(CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_BUS_ID, &config, &ext_config);
#if defined(CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_DMA) && (CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_DMA == 1)
    uapi_dma_init();
    uapi_dma_open();
#ifndef CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_POLL_AND_DMA_AUTO_SWITCH
    spi_dma_config_t dma_cfg = {
        .src_width = SPI_DMA_WIDTH,
        .dest_width = SPI_DMA_WIDTH,
        .burst_length = 0,
        .priority = 0
    };
    if (uapi_spi_set_dma_mode(CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_BUS_ID, true, &dma_cfg) != ERRCODE_SUCC) {
        osal_printk("spi%d slave set dma mode fail!\r\n");
    }
#endif
#endif  /* CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_DMA */

#if defined(CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_INTERRUPT) && (CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_INTERRUPT == 1)
    if (uapi_spi_set_irq_mode(CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_BUS_ID, true, app_spi_slave_rx_callback,
        app_spi_slave_write_int_handler) == ERRCODE_SUCC) {
        osal_printk("spi%d slave set irq mode succ!\r\n", CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_BUS_ID);
    }
#endif  /* CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_INTERRUPT */
}

static void *spi_slave_task(const char *arg)
{
    unused(arg);
    /* SPI pinmux. */
    app_spi_init_pin();

    /* SPI slave init config. */
    app_spi_slave_init_config();

    /* SPI data config. */
    uint8_t tx_data[CONFIG_SPI_TRANSFER_LEN] = { 0 };
    for (uint32_t loop = 0; loop < CONFIG_SPI_TRANSFER_LEN; loop++) {
        tx_data[loop] = (loop & 0xFF);
    }
    uint8_t rx_data[CONFIG_SPI_TRANSFER_LEN] = { 0 };
    spi_xfer_data_t data = {
        .tx_buff = tx_data,
        .tx_bytes = CONFIG_SPI_TRANSFER_LEN,
        .rx_buff = rx_data,
        .rx_bytes = CONFIG_SPI_TRANSFER_LEN,
    };

    while (1) {
        osal_msleep(SPI_TASK_DURATION_MS);
#ifndef CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_SUPPORT_WRITEREAD
        osal_printk("spi%d slave receive start!\r\n", CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_BUS_ID);
        if (uapi_spi_slave_read(CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_BUS_ID, &data, 0xFFFFFFFF) == ERRCODE_SUCC) {
#ifndef CONFIG_SPI_SUPPORT_INTERRUPT
            for (uint32_t i = 0; i < data.rx_bytes; i++) {
                osal_printk("spi%d slave receive data is %x\r\n", CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_BUS_ID, data.rx_buff[i]);
            }
#endif
            osal_printk("spi%d slave receive succ!\r\n", CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_BUS_ID);
        }
        osal_printk("spi%d slave send start!\r\n", CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_BUS_ID);
        if (uapi_spi_slave_write(CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_BUS_ID, &data, 0xFFFFFFFF) == ERRCODE_SUCC) {
            osal_printk("spi%d slave send succ!\r\n", CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_BUS_ID);
        } else {
            continue;
        }
#else
        osal_printk("spi%d slave writeread start!\r\n", CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_BUS_ID);
        if (uapi_spi_slave_writeread(CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_BUS_ID, &data, 0xFFFFFFFF) == ERRCODE_SUCC) {
            for (uint32_t i = 0; i < data.rx_bytes; i++) {
                osal_printk("spi%d slave writeread data is %x\r\n", CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_BUS_ID, data.rx_buff[i]);
            }
            osal_printk("spi%d slave writeread succ!\r\n", CONFIG_SPI_SLAVE_BUS_ID);
        }
#endif
    }

    return NULL;
}

static void spi_slave_entry(void)
{
    osal_task *task_handle = NULL;
    osal_kthread_lock();
    task_handle = osal_kthread_create((osal_kthread_handler)spi_slave_task, 0, "SpiSlaveTask", SPI_TASK_STACK_SIZE);
    if (task_handle != NULL) {
        osal_kthread_set_priority(task_handle, SPI_TASK_PRIO);
    }
    osal_kthread_unlock();
}

/* Run the spi_slave_entry. */
app_run(spi_slave_entry);
```

Pay attention to the following two points when using three-line SPI:

1.  The CS pin of SPI cannot be configured in chip select mode. It must be configured in GPIO mode to output a high level:

    ```
    void spi_set_pinctrl(void)
    {
    #if defined(CONFIG_PINCTRL_SUPPORT_IE)
        uapi_pin_set_ie(CONFIG_MOUSE_PIN_SPI_MISO, PIN_IE_1);
    #endif
        uapi_pin_set_mode(CONFIG_MOUSE_PIN_SPI_MISO, SPI_PIN_MISO_PINMUX);
        uapi_pin_set_mode(CONFIG_MOUSE_PIN_SPI_MOSI, SPI_PIN_MOSI_PINMUX);
        uapi_pin_set_mode(CONFIG_MOUSE_PIN_SPI_CLK, SPI_PIN_CLK_PINMUX);
        uapi_pin_set_pull(CONFIG_MOUSE_PIN_SPI_CLK, PIN_PULL_UP);
        uapi_pin_set_mode(CONFIG_MOUSE_PIN_SPI_CS, HAL_PIO_FUNC_GPIO);
        uapi_gpio_set_dir(CONFIG_MOUSE_PIN_SPI_CS, GPIO_DIRECTION_OUTPUT);
        uapi_gpio_set_val(CONFIG_MOUSE_PIN_SPI_CS, GPIO_LEVEL_HIGH);
    }
    ```

2.  Each time the SPI read/write interface is called, manually change the CS pin level to keep it low during SPI read/write.

    The following uses the master write/read as an example:

    ```
    errcode_t usr_spi_writeread(uint8_t *wdata, uint8_t wlen, uint8_t *rdata, uint8_t rlen)
    {
        spi_xfer_data_t spi_recv_xfer = { 0 };
        spi_recv_xfer.tx_buff = wdata;                               /* Set the TX buffer */
        spi_recv_xfer.tx_bytes = wlen;                               /* Set the TX buffer length */
        spi_recv_xfer.rx_buff = rdata;                               /* Set the RX buffer */
        spi_recv_xfer.rx_bytes = rlen;                               /* Set the RX buffer length */
        spi_porting_set_rx_mode(TEST_SPI, rlen);                     /* Set the RX reception mode of the write/read interface */
        uapi_gpio_set_val(TEST_SPI, GPIO_LEVEL_LOW);                 /* Set the CS pin to low level */
        return uapi_spi_master_writeread(TEST_SPI, &spi_recv_xfer, 100); /* Read data */
        uapi_gpio_set_val(TEST_SPI, GPIO_LEVEL_HIGH);                /* Set the CS pin to high level */
    }
    ```

## Precautions<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713103533"></a>

-   When SPI is no longer used, uapi\_spi\_deinit must be called to release resources. Otherwise, an error will be returned during the next initialization.
-   When the microwire frame protocol is used, the master can only send 8-bit data due to the limitations of the microwire frame protocol.
-   When the chip acts as a master device and the slave device is slow, add an appropriate delay after each read/write interface call to prevent data errors caused by the slave being too slow to read/write data.

# I2C<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001665143690"></a>






## Overview<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713023553"></a>

IIC (Inter-Integrated Circuit), also called I2C, is translated as the inter-integrated circuit bus. It is a serial communication bus that uses a master-slave architecture to facilitate communication between the MCU and peripheral device components.

The I2C bus consists of two lines: SDA (Serial Data Line) and SCL (Serial Clock Line), where SDA is the data line and SCL is the clock line. Each device on the I2C bus has a unique address, through which the master can communicate with the device.

Two groups of I2C peripherals, I2C0 to I2C1, supporting Master/Slave mode are provided. The I2C specifications are as follows:

-   Three working modes are supported: standard, fast, and high-speed. In serial 8-bit bidirectional data transmission scenarios, the standard mode can reach 100 kbit/s, the fast mode can reach 400 kbit/s, and the high-speed mode can reach 3.4 Mbit/s.
-   A FIFO with a bit width of 32 bits x 8 is supported.
-   7-bit/10-bit addressing modes are supported.

## Function Description<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713023509"></a>

>![](public_sys-resources/icon-note.gif) **Note:** 
>If the I2C driver needs to configure DMA mode to read/write data, ensure that the DMA driver has been initialized. For DMA initialization, refer to “[DMA](DMA.md)”.

The interfaces and functions provided by the I2C module are as follows:

-   uapi\_i2c\_master\_init（i2c\_bus\_t bus, uint32\_t baudrate, uint8\_t hscode）：Initialize this I2C device as a master. The parameters to be passed include the bus number, baud rate, and high-speed mode host code (required only in high-speed mode, ranging from 0 to 7)（bus indicates the I2C bus in use，baudrate indicates the I2C baud rate，hscode indicates the I2C high-speed mode host code）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_I2C\_SUPPORT\_MASTER macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_i2c\_master\_write（i2c\_bus\_t bus, uint16\_t dev\_addr, i2c\_data\_t \*data）：The I2C master sends data to the target slave（bus indicates the I2C bus in use，dev\_addr indicates the target slave address，data indicates the data sent by I2C）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_I2C\_SUPPORT\_MASTER macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_i2c\_master\_read（i2c\_bus\_t bus, uint16\_t dev\_addr, i2c\_data\_t \*data）：The master receives data from the target I2C slave（bus indicates the I2C bus in use，dev\_addr indicates the target slave address，data indicates the data received by I2C）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_I2C\_SUPPORT\_MASTER macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_i2c\_master\_writeread（i2c\_bus\_t bus, uint16\_t dev\_addr, i2c\_data\_t \*data）：The master sends data to the target I2C slave and receives data from it（bus indicates the I2C bus in use，dev\_addr indicates the target slave address，data indicates the I2C data configuration）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_I2C\_SUPPORT\_MASTER macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_i2c\_slave\_init（i2c\_bus\_t bus, uint32\_t baudrate, uint16\_t addr）：Initialize this I2C device as a slave. The parameters to be passed include the bus number, baud rate, and slave address (supports 7-bit/10-bit address addressing)（bus indicates the I2C bus in use，baudrate indicates the I2C baud rate，addr indicates the slave address when I2C works as a slave）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_I2C\_SUPPORT\_SLAVE macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_i2c\_slave\_write（i2c\_bus\_t bus, i2c\_data\_t \*data）：The slave sends data to the master（bus indicates the I2C bus in use，data indicates the data sent by I2C）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_I2C\_SUPPORT\_SLAVE macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_i2c\_slave\_read（i2c\_bus\_t bus, i2c\_data\_t \*data）：The slave receives data from the master（bus indicates the I2C bus in use，data indicates the data received by I2C）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_I2C\_SUPPORT\_SLAVE macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_i2c\_deinit（i2c\_bus\_t bus）：Deinitialize the I2C device, supporting both master and slave（bus indicates the I2C bus in use）。
-   uapi\_i2c\_set\_baudrate（i2c\_bus\_t bus, uint32\_t baudrate）：Reset the baud rate of an initialized I2C device, supporting both master and slave（bus indicates the I2C bus in use，baudrate indicates the I2C baud rate）。
-   uapi\_i2c\_set\_dma\_mode（i2c\_bus\_t bus, bool en）：Enable/disable I2C transmission in DMA mode（bus indicates the I2C bus in use，en indicates whether to enable DMA transmission）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_I2C\_SUPPORT\_DMA macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_i2c\_set\_irq\_mode（i2c\_bus\_t bus, bool irq\_en）：Set whether to transmit data in interrupt mode（bus indicates the I2C bus in use，en indicates whether to enable interrupt mode transmission）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_I2C\_SUPPORT\_INT macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_i2c\_register\_irq\_callback（i2c\_bus\_t bus, i2c\_irq\_callback\_t callback）：Register the I2C interrupt event callback function（bus indicates the I2C bus in use，callback indicates the I2C interrupt event callback function）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_I2C\_SUPPORT\_INT macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_i2c\_unregister\_irq\_callback（i2c\_bus\_t bus）：Unregister the I2C interrupt event callback function（bus indicates the I2C bus in use）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_I2C\_SUPPORT\_INT macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_i2c\_suspend（uintptr\_t arg）：Suspend all I2C channels for low-power scenarios（arg indicates the parameters required for suspension）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_I2C\_SUPPORT\_LPM macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_i2c\_resume（uintptr\_t arg）：Resume all I2C channels for low-power scenarios（arg indicates the parameters required for resume）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_I2C\_SUPPORT\_LPM macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_i2c\_master\_writeread\_with\_flag\(i2c\_bus\_t bus, uint16\_t dev\_addr, i2c\_buffer\_wrap\_t \*write\_buffer, i2c\_buffer\_wrap\_t \*read\_buffer\) ：The master sends data to the target I2C slave and receives data from it（bus indicates the I2C bus in use，dev\_addr indicates the target slave address，write\_buffer indicates the information pointer of the data sent by I2C，read\_buffer indicates the information pointer of the data received by I2C）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_I2C\_SUPPORT\_MASTER macro is enabled.)

<th class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="19.878012198780123%" id="mcps1.2.7.1.3"><p id="p18731420115119"><a name="p18731420115119"></a><a name="p18731420115119"></a>Usage scenario</p>
</th>

</th>

<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="19.878012198780123%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.3 "><p id="p182789183235"><a name="p182789183235"></a><a name="p182789183235"></a>I2C supports master selection. Enable it as appropriate.</p>

</tr>

</td>

</tr>

</td>

</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="14.3985601439856%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.6 "><p id="p97001027124119"><a name="p97001027124119"></a><a name="p97001027124119"></a>The default value is 0x3E8. Configure it as appropriate. It is recommended not to modify it.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row962920381394"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="22.62773722627737%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.1 "><p id="p1629738173912"><a name="p1629738173912"></a><a name="p1629738173912"></a>CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_INT</p>

</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="19.878012198780123%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.3 "><p id="p0369111616584"><a name="p0369111616584"></a><a name="p0369111616584"></a>I2C supports interrupt mode. Enable it as appropriate.</p>

</td>

</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="14.3985601439856%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.6 "><p id="p55269547522"><a name="p55269547522"></a><a name="p55269547522"></a>Disabled by default. Enable it as appropriate.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row62681237271"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="22.62773722627737%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.1 "><p id="p112401826112812"><a name="p112401826112812"></a><a name="p112401826112812"></a>CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_IN_CHIP_LOOPBACK</p>

</td>

</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="11.348865113488651%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.5 "><p id="p115505376560"><a name="p115505376560"></a><a name="p115505376560"></a>CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_INT</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="14.3985601439856%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.6 "><p id="p198015734219"><a name="p198015734219"></a><a name="p198015734219"></a>Disabled by default. Enable it as appropriate.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row17817572040"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="22.62773722627737%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.1 "><p id="p109831846175713"><a name="p109831846175713"></a><a name="p109831846175713"></a>CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_DMA</p>
</td>

</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="19.878012198780123%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.3 "><p id="p1164428144915"><a name="p1164428144915"></a><a name="p1164428144915"></a>I2C supports DMA mode. Enable it as appropriate. If you choose the I2C DMA mode, you also need to enable the KCONFIG configuration of the DMA driver. See <a href="KCONFIG配置-27.md">KCONFIG Configuration</a>.</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="10.608939106089391%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.4 "><p id="p17919571246"><a name="p17919571246"></a><a name="p17919571246"></a>Yes</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="11.348865113488651%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.5 "><p id="p151126287424"><a name="p151126287424"></a><a name="p151126287424"></a>CONFIG_DRIVER_SUPPORT_DMA</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="14.3985601439856%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.6 "><p id="p182001731221"><a name="p182001731221"></a><a name="p182001731221"></a>Disabled by default. Enable it as appropriate.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row1071318918131"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="22.62773722627737%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.1 "><p id="p671319910134"><a name="p671319910134"></a><a name="p671319910134"></a>CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_POLL_AND_DMA_AUTO_SWITCH</p>
</td>

</td>

Enable it as appropriate.</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="10.608939106089391%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.4 "><p id="p1571316971311"><a name="p1571316971311"></a><a name="p1571316971311"></a>Yes</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="11.348865113488651%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.5 "><p id="p15555194716423"><a name="p15555194716423"></a><a name="p15555194716423"></a>CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_DMA</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="14.3985601439856%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.6 "><p id="p57965521033"><a name="p57965521033"></a><a name="p57965521033"></a>Disabled by default. Enable it as appropriate.</p>
</td>
</tr>

</td>

</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="19.878012198780123%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.3 "><p id="p1369200125313"><a name="p1369200125313"></a><a name="p1369200125313"></a>Threshold configured when I2C automatically switches between polling and DMA modes. The default value is 16.</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="10.608939106089391%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.4 "><p id="p186635371131"><a name="p186635371131"></a><a name="p186635371131"></a>Yes</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="11.348865113488651%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.5 "><p id="p16793105954210"><a name="p16793105954210"></a><a name="p16793105954210"></a>CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_POLL_AND_DMA_AUTO_SWITCH</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="14.3985601439856%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.6 "><p id="p125242276524"><a name="p125242276524"></a><a name="p125242276524"></a>The default value is 16. Configure it as appropriate.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row1115204491314"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="22.62773722627737%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.1 "><p id="p20115184431314"><a name="p20115184431314"></a><a name="p20115184431314"></a>CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_LPC</p>
</td>

</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="19.878012198780123%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.3 "><p id="p1539110454437"><a name="p1539110454437"></a><a name="p1539110454437"></a>I2C working clock</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="10.608939106089391%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.4 "><p id="p9115124411312"><a name="p9115124411312"></a><a name="p9115124411312"></a>Yes</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="11.348865113488651%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.5 "><p id="p5115544151311"><a name="p5115544151311"></a><a name="p5115544151311"></a>No</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="14.3985601439856%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.6 "><p id="p4131579442"><a name="p4131579442"></a><a name="p4131579442"></a>Enabled by default. Mandatory.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row9686349171316"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="22.62773722627737%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.1 "><p id="p2068610490135"><a name="p2068610490135"></a><a name="p2068610490135"></a>CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_LPM</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="21.137886211378863%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.2 "><p id="p1699112401448"><a name="p1699112401448"></a><a name="p1699112401448"></a>I2C support low power mode, supend and reusme.</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="19.878012198780123%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.3 "><p id="p1435013136441"><a name="p1435013136441"></a><a name="p1435013136441"></a>This macro is used only in low-power scenarios.</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="10.608939106089391%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.4 "><p id="p12686184941312"><a name="p12686184941312"></a><a name="p12686184941312"></a>Yes</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="11.348865113488651%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.5 "><p id="p26871249111313"><a name="p26871249111313"></a><a name="p26871249111313"></a>No</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="14.3985601439856%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.6 "><p id="p10435326114414"><a name="p10435326114414"></a><a name="p10435326114414"></a>Disabled by default. Enable it as appropriate.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row10100123917141"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="22.62773722627737%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.1 "><p id="p6359290154"><a name="p6359290154"></a><a name="p6359290154"></a>CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_CONCURRENCY</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="21.137886211378863%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.2 "><p id="p23825532410"><a name="p23825532410"></a><a name="p23825532410"></a>I2C support concurrency.</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="19.878012198780123%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.3 "><p id="p17131797138"><a name="p17131797138"></a><a name="p17131797138"></a>If this macro is enabled, the integrity of the current transmission can be ensured. The next transmission starts only after the current transmission is complete. This macro must be enabled when no lock is added.</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="10.608939106089391%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.4 "><p id="p131001439161420"><a name="p131001439161420"></a><a name="p131001439161420"></a>Yes</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="11.348865113488651%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.5 "><p id="p110083912148"><a name="p110083912148"></a><a name="p110083912148"></a>No</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="14.3985601439856%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.6 "><p id="p49849494449"><a name="p49849494449"></a><a name="p49849494449"></a>Enabled by default. Recommended to enable.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row9604124541418"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="22.62773722627737%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.1 "><p id="p1198461021518"><a name="p1198461021518"></a><a name="p1198461021518"></a>CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_QUERY_REGS</p>
</td>

</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="19.878012198780123%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.3 "><p id="p438154074512"><a name="p438154074512"></a><a name="p438154074512"></a>I2C supports reading back the values of all related registers. Enable it as appropriate.</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="10.608939106089391%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.4 "><p id="p1860512452145"><a name="p1860512452145"></a><a name="p1860512452145"></a>Yes</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="11.348865113488651%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.5 "><p id="p13605145101411"><a name="p13605145101411"></a><a name="p13605145101411"></a>No</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="14.3985601439856%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.6 "><p id="p1817114179353"><a name="p1817114179353"></a><a name="p1817114179353"></a>Disabled by default. Enable it as appropriate.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row4155553141"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="22.62773722627737%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.1 "><p id="p2119131217152"><a name="p2119131217152"></a><a name="p2119131217152"></a>CONFIG_I2C_USING_V100</p>
</td>

</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="19.878012198780123%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.3 "><p id="p1605194513147"><a name="p1605194513147"></a><a name="p1605194513147"></a>This macro indicates using the I2C V100 driver specification. This specification is not used in this product.</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="10.608939106089391%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.4 "><p id="p201555511146"><a name="p201555511146"></a><a name="p201555511146"></a>Yes</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="11.348865113488651%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.5 "><p id="p1280317457132"><a name="p1280317457132"></a><a name="p1280317457132"></a>No</p>
</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="14.3985601439856%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.6 "><p id="p7644175334613"><a name="p7644175334613"></a><a name="p7644175334613"></a>Disabled by default. It must be disabled.</p>
</td>
</tr>
<tr id="row155854071519"><td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="22.62773722627737%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.1 "><p id="p1289513201519"><a name="p1289513201519"></a><a name="p1289513201519"></a>CONFIG_I2C_USING_V150</p>
</td>

</td>
<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="19.878012198780123%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.3 "><p id="p123612018467"><a name="p123612018467"></a><a name="p123612018467"></a>This macro indicates using the I2C V150 driver specification. This specification is not used in this product.</p>
</td>

## Development Guide<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001665143718"></a>

I2C is used to connect to devices that support the I2C protocol. The I2C unit can work as a master or slave device. The following uses the I2C unit working as a master device as an example:

1.  Configure the pins to be used as I2C functions through IO multiplexing.
2.  Call the uapi\_i2c\_init interface to initialize I2C resources. The following uses initializing the I2C master as an example:

    ```
    #define TEST_I2C                I2C_BUS_0
    #define I2C_BAUDRATE            400000    /* 400kHz */
    #define I2C_PIN_CLK_PINMUX      HAL_PIO_I2C0_CLK
    #define I2C_PIN_DAT_PINMUX      HAL_PIO_I2C0_DATA
    #define I2C_PIN_CLK             S_MGPIO24
    #define I2C_PIN_DAT             S_MGPIO25
    errcode_t sample_i2c_init(void)
    {
        /* Configure i2c pinmux */
        uapi_pin_set_mode(I2C_PIN_CLK, I2C_PIN_CLK_PINMUX);       /* Configure i2c clk pinmux */
        uapi_pin_set_mode(I2C_PIN_DAT, I2C_PIN_DAT_PINMUX);       /* Configure i2c dat pinmux */
        /* Initialize i2c */
        return uapi_i2c_master_init(TEST_I2C, I2C_BAUDRATE, 0);     /* Initialize i2c0 */
    }
    ```

3.  Call the uapi\_i2c\_master\_write interface to enable the master to send data.

    ```
    errcode_t sample_i2c_write(uint8_t *data, uint8_t len, uint16_t addr)
    {
        i2c_data_t i2c_send_data = { 0 };
        i2c_send_data.send_buf = data;                                   /* Set the TX buffer */
        i2c_send_data.send_len = len;                                    /* Set the TX buffer length */
        return uapi_i2c_master_write(TEST_I2C, addr, &i2c_send_data);     /* Send data */
    }
    ```

The complete master/slave send and receive code flow of the two-board I2C in three modes (polling mode, interrupt mode, and DMA mode) can refer to the following code:

```
#include "pinctrl.h"
#include "i2c.h"
#include "soc_osal.h"
#include "app_init.h"
#if defined(CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_DMA) && (CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_DMA == 1)
#include "dma.h"
#endif

#define I2C_MASTER_ADDR                   0x0
#define I2C_SLAVE_ADDR                    0x8
#define I2C_SET_BAUDRATE                  400000
#define I2C_TASK_DURATION_MS              500
#if defined(CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_INT) && (CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_INT == 1)
#define I2C_INT_TRANSFER_DELAY_MS         800
#endif

#define I2C_TASK_PRIO                     24
#define I2C_TASK_STACK_SIZE               0x1000

static i2c_data_t data = { 0 };
static uint8_t tx_buff[CONFIG_I2C_TRANSFER_LEN] = { 0 };
static uint8_t rx_buff[CONFIG_I2C_TRANSFER_LEN] = { 0 };

static void app_i2c_init_pin(void)
{
#if defined(CONFIG_PINCTRL_SUPPORT_IE)
    uapi_pin_set_ie(CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_SCL_PIN, PIN_IE_1);
    uapi_pin_set_ie(CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_SDA_PIN, PIN_IE_1);
#endif /* CONFIG_PINCTRL_SUPPORT_IE */
    /* I2C pinmux. */
    uapi_pin_set_mode(CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_SCL_PIN, CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_SCL_PIN_MODE);
    uapi_pin_set_mode(CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_SDA_PIN, CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_SDA_PIN_MODE);
}

static void app_i2c_data_config(void)
{
    /* I2C data config. */
    for (uint32_t loop = 0; loop < CONFIG_I2C_TRANSFER_LEN; loop++) {
        tx_buff[loop] = (loop & 0xFF);
    }
    data.send_buf = tx_buff;
    data.send_len = CONFIG_I2C_TRANSFER_LEN;
    data.receive_buf = rx_buff;
    data.receive_len = CONFIG_I2C_TRANSFER_LEN;
}

static void *i2c_master_task(const char *arg)
{
    unused(arg);

    uint32_t baudrate = I2C_SET_BAUDRATE;
    uint8_t hscode = I2C_MASTER_ADDR;
    uint16_t dev_addr = I2C_SLAVE_ADDR;

#if defined(CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_DMA) && (CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_DMA == 1)
    uapi_dma_init();
    uapi_dma_open();
#ifndef CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_POLL_AND_DMA_AUTO_SWITCH
    uapi_i2c_set_dma_mode(CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_BUS_ID, true);
#endif
#endif  /* CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_DMA */

    /* I2C master init config. */
    app_i2c_init_pin();
    uapi_i2c_master_init(CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_BUS_ID, baudrate, hscode);

#if defined(CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_INT) && (CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_INT == 1)
    uapi_i2c_set_irq_mode(CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_BUS_ID, true);
#endif  /* CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_INT */

    app_i2c_data_config();

    while (1) {
        osal_msleep(I2C_TASK_DURATION_MS);
#ifndef CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_SUPPORT_WRITEREAD
        osal_printk("i2c%d master send start!\r\n", CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_BUS_ID);
        if (uapi_i2c_master_write(CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_BUS_ID, dev_addr, &data) == ERRCODE_SUCC) {
            osal_printk("i2c%d master send succ!\r\n", CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_BUS_ID);
        } else {
            continue;
        }
#if defined(CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_INT) && (CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_INT == 1)
        osal_msleep(I2C_INT_TRANSFER_DELAY_MS);
#endif
        osal_printk("i2c%d master receive start!\r\n", CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_BUS_ID);
        if (uapi_i2c_master_read(CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_BUS_ID, dev_addr, &data) == ERRCODE_SUCC) {
            for (uint32_t i = 0; i < data.receive_len; i++) {
                osal_printk("i2c%d master receive data is %x\r\n", CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_BUS_ID, data.receive_buf[i]);
            }
            osal_printk("i2c%d master receive succ!\r\n", CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_BUS_ID);
        }
#else
        osal_printk("i2c%d master writeread start!\r\n", CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_BUS_ID);
        if (uapi_i2c_master_writeread(CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_BUS_ID, dev_addr, &data) == ERRCODE_SUCC) {
            for (uint32_t i = 0; i < data.receive_len; i++) {
                osal_printk("i2c%d master writeread data is %x\r\n", CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_BUS_ID, data.receive_buf[i]);
            }
            osal_printk("i2c%d master writeread succ!\r\n", CONFIG_I2C_MASTER_BUS_ID);
        }
#endif
    }

    return NULL;
}

static void i2c_master_entry(void)
{
    osal_task *task_handle = NULL;
    osal_kthread_lock();
    task_handle = osal_kthread_create((osal_kthread_handler)i2c_master_task, 0, "I2cMasterTask", I2C_TASK_STACK_SIZE);
    if (task_handle != NULL) {
        osal_kthread_set_priority(task_handle, I2C_TASK_PRIO);
    }
    osal_kthread_unlock();
}

/* Run the i2c_master_entry. */
app_run(i2c_master_entry);

----------------------------------------------------------------

#include "pinctrl.h"
#include "i2c.h"
#include "soc_osal.h"
#include "app_init.h"
#if defined(CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_DMA) && (CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_DMA == 1)
#include "dma.h"
#endif

#define I2C_SLAVE_ADDR                    0x8
#define I2C_SET_BAUDRATE                  500000
#define I2C_TASK_DURATION_MS              100
#if defined(CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_DMA) && (CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_DMA == 1)
#define I2C_DMA_TRANSFER_DELAY_MS         500
#endif

#define I2C_TASK_PRIO                     24
#define I2C_TASK_STACK_SIZE               0x1000

static void app_i2c_init_pin(void)
{
#if defined(CONFIG_PINCTRL_SUPPORT_IE)
    uapi_pin_set_ie(CONFIG_I2C_SLAVE_SCL_PIN, PIN_IE_1);
    uapi_pin_set_ie(CONFIG_I2C_SLAVE_SDA_PIN, PIN_IE_1);
#endif /* CONFIG_PINCTRL_SUPPORT_IE */
    /* I2C pinmux. */
    uapi_pin_set_mode(CONFIG_I2C_SLAVE_SCL_PIN, CONFIG_I2C_SLAVE_SCL_PIN_MODE);
    uapi_pin_set_mode(CONFIG_I2C_SLAVE_SDA_PIN, CONFIG_I2C_SLAVE_SDA_PIN_MODE);
}

void *i2c_slave_task(const char *arg)
{
    unused(arg);
    i2c_data_t data = { 0 };

    uint32_t baudrate = I2C_SET_BAUDRATE;
    uint16_t dev_addr = I2C_SLAVE_ADDR;

#if defined(CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_DMA) && (CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_DMA == 1)
    uapi_dma_init();
    uapi_dma_open();
#ifndef CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_POLL_AND_DMA_AUTO_SWITCH
    uapi_i2c_set_dma_mode(CONFIG_I2C_SLAVE_BUS_ID, true);
#endif
#endif  /* CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_DMA */

    /* I2C slave init config. */
    app_i2c_init_pin();
    uapi_i2c_slave_init(CONFIG_I2C_SLAVE_BUS_ID, baudrate, dev_addr);

#if defined(CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_INT) && (CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_INT == 1)
    uapi_i2c_set_irq_mode(CONFIG_I2C_SLAVE_BUS_ID, true);
#endif /* CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_INT */

    /* I2C data config. */
    uint8_t tx_buff[CONFIG_I2C_TRANSFER_LEN] = { 0 };
    for (uint32_t loop = 0; loop < CONFIG_I2C_TRANSFER_LEN; loop++) {
        tx_buff[loop] = (loop & 0xFF);
    }

    uint8_t rx_buff[CONFIG_I2C_TRANSFER_LEN] = { 0 };
    data.send_buf = tx_buff;
    data.send_len = CONFIG_I2C_TRANSFER_LEN;
    data.receive_buf = rx_buff;
    data.receive_len = CONFIG_I2C_TRANSFER_LEN;

    while (1) {
        osal_msleep(I2C_TASK_DURATION_MS);
        osal_printk("i2c%d slave receive start!\r\n", CONFIG_I2C_SLAVE_BUS_ID);
        if (uapi_i2c_slave_read(CONFIG_I2C_SLAVE_BUS_ID, &data) == ERRCODE_SUCC) {
            for (uint32_t i = 0; i < data.receive_len; i++) {
                osal_printk("i2c slave receive data is %x\r\n", data.receive_buf[i]);
            }
            osal_printk("i2c%d slave receive succ!\r\n", CONFIG_I2C_SLAVE_BUS_ID);
        } else {
            continue;
        }
        osal_printk("i2c%d slave send start!\r\n", CONFIG_I2C_SLAVE_BUS_ID);
#if defined(CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_DMA) && (CONFIG_I2C_SUPPORT_DMA == 1)
        osal_msleep(I2C_DMA_TRANSFER_DELAY_MS);
#endif
        if (uapi_i2c_slave_write(CONFIG_I2C_SLAVE_BUS_ID, &data) == ERRCODE_SUCC) {
            osal_printk("i2c%d slave send succ!\r\n", CONFIG_I2C_SLAVE_BUS_ID);
        }
    }

    return NULL;
}

static void i2c_slave_entry(void)
{
    osal_task *task_handle = NULL;
    osal_kthread_lock();
    task_handle = osal_kthread_create((osal_kthread_handler)i2c_slave_task, 0, "I2cSlaveTask", I2C_TASK_STACK_SIZE);
    if (task_handle != NULL) {
        osal_kthread_set_priority(task_handle, I2C_TASK_PRIO);
    }
    osal_kthread_unlock();
}

/* Run the i2c_slave_entry. */
app_run(i2c_slave_entry);

```

## Precautions<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001665143726"></a>

-   The uapi\_i2c\_set\_baudrate function must be called after initialization, so that the baud rate can be modified. If the I2C is not initialized and uapi\_i2c\_set\_baudrate is called directly, the error code ERRCODE\_I2C\_NOT\_INIT is returned.
-   Ensure that the data send pointer send\_buf and the data receive pointer receive\_buf are not null pointers.
-   If the data to be sent exceeds the acceptable range of the connected device, sending fails. If sending fails and another I2C device is switched to continue sending, the bus will be suspended, and all I2C devices cannot send data properly.
-   uapi\_i2c\_master\_init cannot be called for initialization multiple times. After use, call uapi\_i2c\_deinit for deinitialization.

# ADC<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001665143710"></a>






## Overview<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713023537"></a>

An ADC (Analog-to-Digital Converter) is a device that converts continuously changing analog signals into discrete digital signals.

Analog signals in the real world, such as temperature, pressure, sound, or images, need to be converted into digital signals that are easier to store, process, and send. An analog-to-digital converter can implement this function and can be applied to battery level detection, key detection, and so on.

Two modes are provided for developers to choose from: GAFE (General Analog Front End) and AAFE (Audio Analog Front End):

-   GAFE: 8-channel 13-bit 1.6 Msps SAR ADC, providing oversampling and buffer functions. It is used in low-frequency single-ended/differential measurement scenarios such as Tag-ID, battery voltage measurement, and NTC temperature measurement.
-   AAFE: 40 dB pre-amplification, reusing the SAR ADC, with the data domain downsampled to 16 ksps or 32 ksps. It is suitable for analog microphone sampling scenarios.

## Function Description<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713023585"></a>

The interfaces and functions provided by the ADC module are as follows:

-   uapi\_adc\_init（adc\_clock\_t clock）：Initializes the ADC module（clock indicates the clock for ADC sampling）.
-   uapi\_adc\_deinit（void）：Deinitializes the ADC.
-   uapi\_adc\_power\_en（afe\_scan\_mode\_t afe\_scan\_mode, bool en）：Powers on/off and enables or disables the ADC（afe\_scan\_mode indicates the AFE precision mode to use，en indicates the power-on or power-off state）.
-   uapi\_adc\_is\_using（void）：Checks whether the ADC is in use.
-   adc\_calibration: ADC calibration, including 4-step calibration.
-   uapi\_adc\_open\_channel（uint8\_t channel）：Opens an ADC channel\(applicable to all channels\)（channel indicates the ADC channel to open）.
-   uapi\_adc\_close\_channel（uint8\_t channel）：Closes an ADC channel\(applicable to all channels\)（channel indicates the ADC channel to close）.
-   uapi\_adc\_open\_differential\_channel（uint8\_t postive\_ch, uint8\_t negative\_ch）：Opens a differential ADC channel\(applicable to GADC\_CHANNEL\_6 and GADC\_CHANNEL\_7\)（postive\_ch indicates the ADC positive channel，negative\_ch indicates the ADC negative channel）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_ADC\_SUPPORT\_DIFFERENTIAL macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_adc\_close\_differential\_channel（uint8\_t postive\_ch, uint8\_t negative\_ch）：Closes a differential ADC channel\(applicable to GADC\_CHANNEL\_6 and GADC\_CHANNEL\_7\)（postive\_ch indicates the ADC positive channel，negative\_ch indicates the ADC negative channel）。(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_ADC\_SUPPORT\_DIFFERENTIAL macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_adc\_auto\_sample（uint8\_t channel）：ADC sampling interface. The reported value is the code word value relative to the reference voltage\(a 32-bit signed number\), which needs to be converted into a voltage value using a formula（channel indicates the ADC channel）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_ADC\_SUPPORT\_AFE macro is enabled.)
-   adc\_port\_gadc\_entirely\_open\(adc\_channel\_t channel, bool self\_cali\)：Includes the whole flow of initializing a GADC channel. The self\_cali parameter indicates whether to calibrate the ADC during this initialization. If calibration is required, it takes about 170 ms. If the calibration value has been stored, you can pass false.
-   adc\_port\_gadc\_entirely\_sample\(adc\_channel\_t channel\)：Includes the whole flow of sampling a GADC channel. The return value is the voltage value converted to mV.
-   adc\_port\_gadc\_entirely\_close\(adc\_channel\_t channel\)：Includes the whole flow of closing a GADC channel and records the current calibration value.

## Development Guide<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001665143746"></a>

The configurable items are mainly in the porting-layer header file corresponding to the ADC. The following describes the functions of important configurable items.

GAFE:

-   cfg\_adc\_data0.b.data\_num: In counting mode, the number of sampling points is 2<sup>x</sup>.
-   cfg\_adc\_data0.b.wait\_num: The number of sampling values discarded while waiting for the data link to stabilize.
-   cfg\_adc\_data1.b.osr\_len: The number of accumulated samples in oversampling.
-   cfg\_adc\_data1.b.osr\_sel: The number of averaged samples in oversampling. It is configured to the same value as the number of accumulated samples. The reported value is (data1+data2+...+data\_osr\_len)/osr\_sel.
-   To shorten the GADC sampling time, you can reduce the values of the preceding four variables. A set of limit values is given here:
-   .cfg\_adc\_data0.b.data\_num = 0xa,

    .cfg\_adc\_data0.b.wait\_num = 4,

    .cfg\_adc\_data1.b.osr\_len= 2,

    .cfg\_adc\_data1.b.osr\_sel= 2,

AAFE:

-   .pga\_gain: The PGA gain multiplier of the pre-amplifier of the AAFE circuit.

Example:

GAFE enable:

```
static void test_afe_set_io(uint8_t channel)
{
    pin_t adc_pin[] = {S_MGPIO2, S_MGPIO3, S_MGPIO4, S_MGPIO5, S_MGPIO28, S_MGPIO29, S_MGPIO30, S_MGPIO31};
    uapi_pin_set_mode(adc_pin[channel], PIN_MODE_0);
    uapi_gpio_set_dir(adc_pin[channel], GPIO_DIRECTION_INPUT);
    uapi_pin_set_pull(adc_pin[channel], PIN_PULL_NONE);
    uapi_pin_set_ie(adc_pin[channel], PIN_IE_1);
}
int gadc_open_demo(void)
{
)   uint8_t channel = GADC_CHANNEL_0;
    if (channel >= ADC_CHANNEL_MAX_NUM) {
        return ERROR_BAD_PARAMS;
    }
    test_afe_set_io(channel);
    uapi_adc_init(ADC_CLOCK_NONE);
    uapi_adc_power_en(AFE_GADC_MODE, true);
    uapi_adc_open_channel(channel);
    adc_calibration(AFE_GADC_MODE, true, true, true);
    return TEST_OK;
}

```

GAFE sampling:

```
#define ADC_AUTO_SAMPLE_TEST_TIMES 10
int adc_auto_sample_demo(void)
{
    adc_v153_gadc_channel_sel_t channel = GADC_CHANNEL_1;  // Select the channel to sample during sampling
    uapi_adc_open_channel(channel);
    int adc_value = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < ADC_AUTO_SAMPLE_TEST_TIMES; i++) {
        adc_value =  adc_port_gadc_entirely_sample(channel);
        test_suite_log_stringf("adc:%dmV%x\n", adc_value);
    }
    return TEST_OK;
}

```

GAFE channel switching sampling:

```
int adc_auto_sample_demo(void)
{
    adc_v153_gadc_channel_sel_t channel = GADC_CHANNEL_1;  // Select the channel to sample during sampling
 
    int adc_value = 0;
    for (int i = 0; i < ADC_AUTO_SAMPLE_TEST_TIMES; i++) {
        adc_value =  adc_port_gadc_entirely_sample(channel);
        test_suite_log_stringf("adc: %dmV%x\n", adc_value);
    }
    channel = GADC_CHANNEL_3;  // Switch to the next channel to sample
    for (int i = 0; i < ADC_AUTO_SAMPLE_TEST_TIMES; i++) {
        adc_value =  adc_port_gadc_entirely_sample(channel);
        test_suite_log_stringf("adc: %dmV%x\n", adc_value);
    }
    return TEST_OK;
}

Using the GAFE entire interfaces:
void adc_entire_demo(void) {
    uint8_t gadc_channel1 = GADC_CHANNEL_0;
    uint8_t gadc_channel2 = GADC_CHANNEL_5;
    int adc_value = 0;
    adc_port_gadc_entirely_open(gadc_channel1,true); // First time the ADC is enabled, pass true to indicate this calibration.
    adc_value =  adc_port_gadc_entirely_sample(gadc_channel1);
    osal_printk("gadc: %dmv\n", adc_value );
    adc_port_gadc_entirely_close(gadc_channel1);    // The calibration value is recorded when it is closed
    adc_port_gadc_entirely_open(gadc_channel1, false);  // False indicates that the recorded calibration value is used for this enabling.
    adc_value =  adc_port_gadc_entirely_sample(gadc_channel2);
    osal_printk("gadc_channel2: %dmv\n", adc_value);
    adc_port_gadc_entirely_close(gadc_channel2);
}

```

```
AAFE enable & sampling:
int amic_open_demo(void)
{
    test_afe_set_io(AMIC_CHANNEL_0);
    test_afe_set_io(AMIC_CHANNEL_1);
    uapi_adc_init(ADC_CLOCK_NONE);
    uapi_adc_power_en(AFE_AMIC_MODE, true);
    uapi_adc_open_differential_channel(AMIC_CHANNEL_0, AMIC_CHANNEL_1);
    adc_calibration(AFE_AMIC_MODE, true, true, true);
    uapi_adc_auto_sample(AMIC_CHANNEL_0);  // After sampling is enabled, the data needs to be moved away by the audio driver
    return TEST_OK;
}
```

## Precautions<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713103493"></a>

-   GAFE analog input voltage range.

    Due to the limitation of the GPIO supply voltage for digital-analog multiplexing, the ADC reference voltage is 1.5 V. Therefore, the eight ports can only accept 0\~1.5 V input. Voltages above 1.5 V are forbidden. For higher voltages, it is recommended to divide the voltage to within 1.5 V using external resistors before connecting to the GAFE input. **Before developing a board with a voltage divider circuit, communicate with the hardware engineer**. After adding the voltage divider circuit, capacitance may introduce charge/discharge time, which needs to be determined through actual measurement.

-   Method for processing audio signals collected by AAFE.

    The audio signal collected through uapi\_adc\_auto\_sample has the same format as the battery signal collected by GAFE, including 13 valid bits and 1 sign bit. The processed value is sent through the PDM module. For the specific implementation, see the "[Development Guide](开发指引-77.md)" section.

-   It is recommended to use the three interfaces with entirely, which is simpler. Calibrate during the first initialization after power-on.

# DMA<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713023505"></a>






## Overview<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001665143702"></a>

DMA (Direct Memory Access) is a working mode in which data exchange is entirely executed by hardware. In this mode, the DMA controller (DMAC) directly transfers data between memory and peripherals, between peripherals, and between memory and memory, reducing processor intervention and overhead.

The DMA mode is generally used for high-speed transmission of grouped data. After receiving a DMA transfer request, the DMAC starts the bus master controller according to the CPU configuration of the channel, sends address and control signals to the memory and peripherals, counts the number of transferred data items, and reports the completion or error of the transfer operation to the CPU by means of an interrupt.

The DMA specifications are as follows:

-   Memory-to-memory, memory-to-peripheral, peripheral-to-memory, and peripheral-to-peripheral transfer types are supported.
-   The MCU-side DMA supports 8 channels and 16 hardware handshaking interfaces, and the channel parameter priority is configurable.
-   All channels support a maximum single-packet length of 4095 bytes of data. 4K data must be within the same sector.
-   The endian is configurable.

## Function Description<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713103545"></a>

>![](public_sys-resources/icon-note.gif) **Note:** 
>If DMA is needed to transfer data in SPI/UART/I2C/I2S/PDM, DMA initialization must be performed during system startup.

The interfaces and functions provided by the DMA module are as follows:

-   uapi\_dma\_init（void）：Initializes the DMA.
-   uapi\_dma\_deinit（void）：Deinitializes the DMA.
-   uapi\_dma\_open（void）：Opens the DMA.
-   uapi\_dma\_close（void）：Closes the DMA.
-   uapi\_dma\_start\_transfer（uint8\_t channel）：Starts DMA transfer on the specified channel（channel indicates the DMA channel number to open）.
-   uapi\_dma\_end\_transfer（uint8\_t channel）：Stops DMA transfer on the specified channel（channel indicates the DMA channel number to stop）.
-   uapi\_dma\_get\_block\_ts（uint8\_t channel）：Obtains the amount of data transferred by DMA（channel indicates the DMA channel number）.
-   uapi\_dma\_transfer\_memory\_single（const dma\_ch\_user\_memory\_config\_t \*user\_cfg, dma\_transfer\_cb\_t callback, uintptr\_t arg）：Transfers memory-to-memory data through the DMA channel（user\_cfg indicates the DMA channel transfer configuration，callback indicates the channel transfer completion callback function，arg indicates the parameter passed back to the callback function when the transfer is complete）.
-   uapi\_dma\_configure\_peripheral\_transfer\_single（const dma\_ch\_user\_peripheral\_config\_t \*user\_cfg, uint8\_t \*channel, dma\_transfer\_cb\_t callback, uintptr\_t arg）：Transfers memory-to-peripheral or peripheral-to-memory data through the DMA channel（user\_cfg indicates the DMA channel transfer configuration，channel indicates the selected DMA channel number，callback indicates the channel transfer completion callback function，arg indicates the parameter passed back to the callback function when the transfer is complete）.
-   uapi\_dma\_get\_lli\_channel（uint8\_t burst\_length, uint8\_t handshaking）：Obtains the DMA linked-list transfer channel（burst\_length indicates the DMA burst transfer length，handshaking indicates the DMA transfer peripheral type）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_DMA\_SUPPORT\_LLI macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_dma\_transfer\_memory\_lli（uint8\_t channel, const dma\_ch\_user\_memory\_config\_t \*user\_cfg, dma\_transfer\_cb\_t callback）：Transfers memory-to-memory data through the DMA channel in linked-list mode（channel indicates the DMA channel number，user\_cfg indicates the DMA channel transfer configuration，callback indicates the channel transfer completion callback function）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_DMA\_SUPPORT\_LLI macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_dma\_configure\_peripheral\_transfer\_lli（uint8\_t channel, const dma\_ch\_user\_peripheral\_config\_t \*user\_cfg, dma\_transfer\_cb\_t callback）：Transfers memory-to-peripheral or peripheral-to-memory data through the DMA channel in linked-list mode（channel indicates the DMA channel number，user\_cfg indicates the DMA channel transfer configuration，callback indicates the channel transfer completion callback function）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_DMA\_SUPPORT\_LLI macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_dma\_enbale\_lli（uint8\_t channel, dma\_transfer\_cb\_t callback, uintptr\_t arg）：Enables DMA linked-list transfer（channel indicates the DMA channel number，callback indicates the channel transfer completion callback function，arg indicates the parameter passed back to the callback function when the transfer is complete）.

    (It can be used only when the CONFIG\_DMA\_SUPPORT\_LLI macro is enabled.)

## Development Guide<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001665143686"></a>

The DMA interface only provides the memory-to-memory copy function to the outside (for other copy modes, see the development guide of the corresponding peripheral driver in this document). The operation steps are as follows:

1.  Call the uapi\_dma\_init interface to initialize the DMA module.
2.  Call the uapi\_dma\_open interface to open the DMA channel.
3.  Call the uapi\_dma\_transfer interface to start DMA transfer. The block parameter can be used to set whether it is blocking mode.

Example:

```
#include "dma.h"
#include "hal_dma.h"
/* Process the callback function after the transfer is complete */
static bool g_dma_trans_done;
static bool g_dma_trans_succ;
void test_dma_trans_done_callback(uint8_t intr, uint8_t channel, uintptr_t arg)
{
    unused(channel);
    unused(arg);
    switch (intr) {
        case HAL_DMA_INTERRUPT_TFR:
            g_dma_trans_done = true;
            g_dma_trans_succ = true;
            break;
        case HAL_DMA_INTERRUPT_BLOCK:
            g_dma_trans_done = true;
            g_dma_trans_succ = true;
            break;
        case HAL_DMA_INTERRUPT_ERR:
            g_dma_trans_done = true;
            g_dma_trans_succ = false;
            break;
        default:
            break;
    }
    osal_printk("[DMA] int_type is %d. \r\n", intr);
}
static void test_fill_test_buffer(void *data, unsigned int length)
{
    for (unsigned int i = 0; i < length; i++) {
        *((unsigned char *)data + i) = (unsigned char)i;
    }
}
static void test_clear_test_buffer(void *data, unsigned int length)
{
    memset_s(data, length, 0, length);
}
errcode_t test_dma_mem_to_mem_single(void)
{
    dma_ch_user_memory_config_t transfer_config;
    /* Fill the data to be sent to the source address */
    test_fill_test_buffer((void *)(uintptr_t)g_dma_src_data, sizeof(g_dma_src_data));
    /* Clear the data at the destination address */
    test_clear_test_buffer((void *)(uintptr_t)g_dma_desc_data, sizeof(g_dma_desc_data));
    /* Initialize the DMA */
    uapi_dma_init();
    /* Open the DMA module */
    uapi_dma_open();
    /* Source address */
    transfer_config.src = ((uint32_t)(uintptr_t)g_dma_src_data);
    /* Destination address */
    transfer_config.dest = ((uint32_t)(uintptr_t)g_dma_desc_data);
    /* Transfer number */
    transfer_config.transfer_num = 100;
    /* Priority 0-3, 0 is the lowest */
    transfer_config.priority = 0;
    /* Transfer width 0: 1 byte 1: 2 bytes 2: 4 bytes */
    transfer_config.width = 0;
    /* Call the block-send interface and register the callback function */
    if (uapi_dma_transfer_memory_single(&transfer_config, test_dma_trans_done_callback, 0) != ERRCODE_SUCC) {
        return ERRCODE_FAIL;
    }
    /* Wait for sending to complete */
    while (!g_dma_trans_done) { }
    if (!g_dma_trans_succ) {
        return ERRCODE_FAIL;
    }
    return ERRCODE_SUCC;
}

The complete DMA code flow can refer to the following code:
#include "hal_dma.h"
#include "soc_osal.h"
#include "securec.h"
#include "app_init.h"
#include "dma.h"

#define DMA_TRANSFER_WORD_NUM       32
#define DMA_TRANSFER_PRIORITY       0
#define DMA_TRANSFER_WIDTH          2
#define DMA_TASK_DURATION_MS        500

#define DMA_TASK_PRIO               24
#define DMA_TASK_STACK_SIZE         0x1000

static uint32_t g_app_dma_src_data[DMA_TRANSFER_WORD_NUM] = { 0 };
static uint32_t g_app_dma_desc_data[DMA_TRANSFER_WORD_NUM] = { 0 };
static uint8_t g_dma_trans_done = 0;

static void app_dma_trans_done_callback(uint8_t int_type, uint8_t channel, uintptr_t arg)
{
    unused(arg);
    unused(channel);
    switch (int_type) {
        case HAL_DMA_INTERRUPT_TFR:
            g_dma_trans_done = 1;
            break;
        case HAL_DMA_INTERRUPT_BLOCK:
            g_dma_trans_done = 1;
            break;
        case HAL_DMA_INTERRUPT_ERR:
            osal_printk("DMA transfer error.\r\n");
            break;
        default:
            break;
    }
}

static void *dma_task(const char *arg)
{
    unused(arg);
    /* DMA init. */
    uapi_dma_deinit();
    uapi_dma_init();
    uapi_dma_open();

#if defined(CONFIG_DMA_MEMORY_LLI_TRANSFER_MODE)
    dma_channel_t dma_channel = DMA_CHANNEL_NONE;
    /* The following two input parameters need to be adapted according to the actual situation when using SMDMA. */
    dma_channel = uapi_dma_get_lli_channel(0, HAL_DMA_HANDSHAKING_MAX_NUM);
#endif

    for (uint32_t i = 0; i < DMA_TRANSFER_WORD_NUM; i++) {
        g_app_dma_src_data[i] = i;
    }
    memset_s(g_app_dma_desc_data, DMA_TRANSFER_WORD_NUM, 0, DMA_TRANSFER_WORD_NUM);

    dma_ch_user_memory_config_t transfer_config = { 0 };
    transfer_config.src = (uint32_t)(uintptr_t)g_app_dma_src_data;
    transfer_config.dest = (uint32_t)(uintptr_t)g_app_dma_desc_data;
    transfer_config.transfer_num = DMA_TRANSFER_WORD_NUM;
    transfer_config.priority = DMA_TRANSFER_PRIORITY;
    transfer_config.width = DMA_TRANSFER_WIDTH;

    while (1) {
        osal_msleep(DMA_TASK_DURATION_MS);
        g_dma_trans_done = 0;
#if defined(CONFIG_DMA_MEMORY_LLI_TRANSFER_MODE)
/* Here, only one node is configured in the linked list and the memory-to-memory transfer is performed. For the interfaces used for peripheral-to-memory transfer, refer to the sample code of other drivers */
        osal_printk("dma config link list item of memory to memory start!\r\n");
        if (uapi_dma_transfer_memory_lli(dma_channel, &transfer_config, app_dma_trans_done_callback) == ERRCODE_SUCC) {
            osal_printk("dma config link list item of memory to memory succ!\r\n");
        }
        osal_printk("dma enable lli memory transfer start!\r\n");
        if (uapi_dma_enable_lli(dma_channel, app_dma_trans_done_callback, (uintptr_t)NULL) == ERRCODE_SUCC) {
            osal_printk("dma enable lli memory transfer succ!\r\n");
        }
        while (!g_dma_trans_done) {}
        if (uapi_dma_end_transfer(dma_channel) == ERRCODE_SUCC) {
            osal_printk("dma channel transfer finish!\r\n");
        }
#else
        osal_printk("dma single memory transfer start!\r\n");
        if (uapi_dma_transfer_memory_single(&transfer_config, app_dma_trans_done_callback,
                                            (uintptr_t)NULL) == ERRCODE_SUCC) {
            osal_printk("dma single memory transfer succ!\r\n");
        }
        while (!g_dma_trans_done) {}
        osal_printk("dma checking transfer from 0x%08x to 0x%08x...\r\n", transfer_config.src, transfer_config.dest);
        if (memcmp((void *)transfer_config.src, (void *)transfer_config.dest, transfer_config.transfer_num) == 0) {
            osal_printk("dma memory copy test succ, length = %d block\r\n", transfer_config.transfer_num);
        }
#endif
    }

    return NULL;
}

static void dma_entry(void)
{
    osal_task *task_handle = NULL;
    osal_kthread_lock();
    task_handle = osal_kthread_create((osal_kthread_handler)dma_task, 0, "DmaTask", DMA_TASK_STACK_SIZE);
    if (task_handle != NULL) {
        osal_kthread_set_priority(task_handle, DMA_TASK_PRIO);
    }
    osal_kthread_unlock();
}

/* Run the dma_entry. */
app_run(dma_entry);

```

## Precautions<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713023549"></a>

-   It is recommended to use DMA only in scenarios where non-blocking data copy is required. In this case, the CPU can be released. After the transfer is complete, the CPU reports an interrupt, and the transfer success or failure can be determined in the callback function based on the event type. In blocking transfer scenarios, it is still recommended to use memcpy\_s for data copy.
-   The DMA IDLE interrupt is not triggered when the FIFO is empty. It is triggered only when the FIFO has data but the waterline is not reached within a period of time. Therefore, even if the waterline is set to 1 byte, the IDLE interrupt is not triggered.

# PWM<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713103501"></a>






## Overview<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713023561"></a>

PWM (Pulse Width Modulation) modulates the width of a series of pulses to produce the required waveform equivalently. That is, the analog signal level is digitally encoded, and the signal changes are adjusted by adjusting the frequency and duty cycle.

The PWM specifications are as follows:

-   12 channels of PWM output are supported, and the registers can be configured separately.
-   PWM grouping is supported. Multiple channels can be allocated to one group, and the same channel cannot be in different groups at the same time.
-   Both the low-level width and the high-level width are adjustable.
-   A fixed-cycle-count sending mode is supported.
-   Transfer-complete interrupts, interrupt clearing, and interrupt query are supported.
-   Only the low 16-bit phase-control counting, frequency-control counting, and duty-cycle counting are supported.

## Function Description<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713103505"></a>

The interfaces and functions provided by the PWM module are as follows:

-   uapi\_pwm\_init（void）：Initializes the PWM.
-   uapi\_pwm\_deinit（void）：Deinitializes the PWM.
-   uapi\_pwm\_open（uint8\_t channel, const pwm\_config\_t \*cfg）：Opens a PWM channel（channel indicates the PWM channel number，cfg indicates the configuration information of the PWM device）.
-   uapi\_pwm\_close（uint8\_t channel）：Closes a PWM channel（channel indicates the PWM channel number）.
-   uapi\_pwm\_get\_frequency（uint8\_t channel）：Obtains the PWM working frequency（channel indicates the PWM channel number）.
-   uapi\_pwm\_start（uint8\_t channel）：Starts PWM signal output（channel indicates the PWM channel number）.
-   uapi\_pwm\_isr（uint8\_t channel）：PWM interrupt service routine（channel indicates the PWM channel number）.
-   uapi\_pwm\_register\_interrupt（uint8\_t channel, pwm\_callback\_t callback）：Registers an interrupt callback for the PWM（channel indicates the PWM channel number，callback indicates the callback function called when the PWM channel transfer is complete）.
-   uapi\_pwm\_unregister\_interrupt（uint8\_t channel）：Deregisters the interrupt callback of the PWM（channel indicates the PWM channel number）.
-   uapi\_pwm\_set\_group（uint8\_t group, const uint8\_t \*channel\_set, uint32\_t channel\_set\_len）：Groups PWM channels（group indicates the number of PWM groups，channel\_set indicates the channel set to be grouped，channel\_set\_len indicates the length of the channel set to be grouped）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_PWM\_USING\_V151 macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_pwm\_clear\_group（uint8\_t group）：Clears the PWM channel groups（group indicates the number of PWM groups）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_PWM\_USING\_V151 macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_pwm\_start\_group（uint8\_t group）：Starts the PWM of the specified group（group indicates the number of PWM groups）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_PWM\_USING\_V151 macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_pwm\_stop\_group（uint8\_t group）：Stops the PWM of the specified group（group indicates the number of PWM groups）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_PWM\_USING\_V151 macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_pwm\_update\_cfg（uint8\_t channel, const pwm\_config\_t \*cfg）：Updates the configuration of the specified PWM channel（channel indicates the PWM channel number，cfg indicates the configuration information of the PWM device）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_PWM\_USING\_V151 macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_pwm\_config\_preload（uint8\_t group, uint8\_t channel, const pwm\_config\_t \*cfg）：PWM preconfiguration. When the previous PWM configuration is complete, this configuration is automatically loaded（group indicates the number of PWM groups，channel indicates the PWM channel number，cfg indicates the configuration information of the PWM device）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_PWM\_USING\_V151 and CONFIG\_PWM\_PRELOAD macros are enabled.)
-   uapi\_pwm\_suspend（uintptr\_t arg）：Suspends all PWM channels for low-power scenarios（arg indicates the parameters required for suspension）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_PWM\_SUPPORT\_LPM macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_pwm\_resume（uintptr\_t arg）：Resumes all PWM channels for low-power scenarios（arg indicates the parameters required for resume）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_PWM\_SUPPORT\_LPM macro is enabled.)
