## Development Guide<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713103561"></a>

The PWM uses the digital output of the microprocessor to control analog circuits. The operation steps are as follows:

1.  Call the uapi\_pin\_set\_mode interface to configure the IO as a PWM function through multiplexing.
2.  Call uapi\_pwm\_init to initialize the PWM.
3.  Call uapi\_pwm\_set\_group to group the PWM.
4.  Call uapi\_pwm\_open to configure PWM parameters and open the specified channel.
5.  Call the uapi\_pwm\_register\_interrupt interface to register the callback function of the PWM interrupt.
6.  Call the uapi\_pwm\_start or uapi\_pwm\_start\_group interface to enable PWM signal output of the specified ID or the specified group.
7.  Call the uapi\_pwm\_stop\_group interface to stop PWM signal output of the specified group.
8.  Call the uapi\_pwm\_close interface to close PWM signal output of the specified ID.
9.  Call the uapi\_pwm\_deinit interface to deinitialize the PWM module.

Example:

```
#include "pwm.h"
#include "pwm_porting.h"
#define TEST_MAX_TIMES 10
#define TEST_DELAY_MS 1000
#define GROUP_ID      0
/* Register the interrupt callback function of the PWM */
static errcode_t pwm_test_callback(pwm_channel_t channel)
{
    osal_printk("PWM channel number is %d, func of interrupt start. \r\n", channel);
    uapi_pwm_isr(channel);
    return ERRCODE_SUCC;
}
void test_pwm_sample(pin_t pin, pin_mode_t mode, pwm_channel_t channel)
{
    /* Set the number of loops */
    unsigned int test_times;
    /* Configure low_time, high_time, cycles, repeat. When repeat is true, cycles is invalid */
    /* offset_time is not used, so configure it to 0 */
    pwm_config_t cfg_repeat = { 100, 100, 0, 0, true };
    /* Set the mode that can be used as a PWM IO */
    uapi_pin_set_mode(pin, mode);
    uapi_pwm_init();
    uint8_t channel_id = channel;
    uapi_pwm_set_group(GROUP_ID, channel_id, 1);
    /* Open the PWM of the specified channel */
    uapi_pwm_open(channel, &cfg_repeat);
    /* Register the callback function */
    uapi_pwm_register_interrupt(channel, pwm_test_callback);
    /* Start the pwm output of the specified channel */
    uapi_pwm_start(channel);
    /* Currently set to cyclic output. Loop TEST_MAX_TIMES times, delay TEST_DELAY_MS each time, then close the pwm output */
    for (test_times = 0; test_times <= TEST_MAX_TIMES; test_times++) {
    if (test_times == TEST_MAX_TIMES) {
            uapi_pwm_stop_group(GROUP_ID);
            uapi_pwm_close(channel);
            osal_printk("now close the pwm output and trigger interrupt \r\n");
        }
        osal_mdelay(TEST_DELAY_MS);
    }
    uapi_pwm_deinit();
    return;
}

The complete PWM code flow can refer to the following code:
#if defined(CONFIG_PWM_SUPPORT_LPM)
#include "pm_veto.h"
#endif
#include "common_def.h"
#include "pinctrl.h"
#include "pwm.h"
#include "tcxo.h"
#include "soc_osal.h"
#include "app_init.h"

#define TEST_TCXO_DELAY_500MS      500
#define PWM_LOW_TIME_CYC           0
#define PWM_HIGH_TIME_CYC          20
#define PWM_TASK_PRIO              24
#define PWM_TASK_STACK_SIZE        0x1000

static uint32_t g_pwm_cyc_done_cnt = 0;
static errcode_t pwm_sample_callback(uint8_t channel)
{
    unused(channel);
    g_pwm_cyc_done_cnt++;
    return ERRCODE_SUCC;
}

void pwm_repeat_mode(void)
{
    pwm_config_t cfg_repeat = {
        PWM_LOW_TIME_CYC,
        PWM_HIGH_TIME_CYC,
        0,
        0xFF,
        true
    };

    uapi_pwm_init();
    uapi_pwm_open(CONFIG_PWM_CHANNEL, &cfg_repeat);
    uapi_pwm_register_interrupt(CONFIG_PWM_CHANNEL, pwm_sample_callback);
#if defined(CONFIG_PWM_SUPPORT_LPM)
    /* veto sleep, or pwm will stop after sleep */
    uapi_pm_add_sleep_veto(PM_USER0_VETO_ID);
#endif
#ifdef CONFIG_PWM_USING_V151
    uint8_t channel_id = CONFIG_PWM_CHANNEL;
    uapi_pwm_set_group(CONFIG_PWM_GROUP_ID, &channel_id, 1);
    uapi_pwm_start_group(CONFIG_PWM_GROUP_ID);
#else
    uapi_pwm_start(CONFIG_PWM_CHANNEL);
#endif
    uapi_tcxo_delay_ms((uint32_t)TEST_TCXO_DELAY_500MS);

#ifdef CONFIG_PWM_USING_V151
    /* update duty ratio config */
    while (cfg_repeat.low_time <= PWM_HIGH_TIME_CYC) {
        uapi_pwm_update_cfg(CONFIG_PWM_CHANNEL, &cfg_repeat);
        uapi_tcxo_delay_ms((uint32_t)TEST_TCXO_DELAY_500MS);
        osal_printk("pwm cyc_done_cnt:%u, duty_radio:[%u/%u]\r\n", g_pwm_cyc_done_cnt,
            cfg_repeat.high_time, cfg_repeat.high_time + cfg_repeat.low_time);
        cfg_repeat.low_time++;
        cfg_repeat.high_time--;
    }
    uapi_pwm_stop_group(CONFIG_PWM_GROUP_ID);
#endif

#if defined(CONFIG_PWM_SUPPORT_LPM)
    uapi_pm_remove_sleep_veto(PM_USER0_VETO_ID);  /* remove veto sleep */
#endif
    uapi_pwm_close(CONFIG_PWM_CHANNEL);
    uapi_pwm_deinit();
}

static void *pwm_task(const char *arg)
{
    unused(arg);
    pwm_config_t cfg_no_repeat = {
        100,
        100,
        0,
        0xFF,
        false
    };

    uapi_pin_set_mode(CONFIG_PWM_PIN, CONFIG_PWM_PIN_MODE);
    uapi_pwm_init();
    uapi_pwm_open(CONFIG_PWM_CHANNEL, &cfg_no_repeat);
    uapi_pwm_register_interrupt(CONFIG_PWM_CHANNEL, pwm_sample_callback);
#ifdef CONFIG_PWM_USING_V151
    uint8_t channel_id = CONFIG_PWM_CHANNEL;
    /* channel_id can also choose to configure multiple channels, and the third parameter also needs to be adjusted
        accordingly. */
    uapi_pwm_set_group(CONFIG_PWM_GROUP_ID, &channel_id, 1);
    /* Here you can also call the uapi_pwm_start interface to open each channel individually. */
    uapi_pwm_start_group(CONFIG_PWM_GROUP_ID);
#else
    uapi_pwm_start(CONFIG_PWM_CHANNEL);
#endif

    uapi_tcxo_delay_ms((uint32_t)TEST_TCXO_DELAY_500MS);
    uapi_pwm_close(CONFIG_PWM_CHANNEL);
    uapi_pwm_deinit();

    /* pwm repeat mode */
    pwm_repeat_mode();

    return NULL;
}

static void pwm_entry(void)
{
    osal_task *task_handle = NULL;
    osal_kthread_lock();
    task_handle = osal_kthread_create((osal_kthread_handler)pwm_task, 0, "PwmTask", PWM_TASK_STACK_SIZE);
    if (task_handle != NULL) {
        osal_kthread_set_priority(task_handle, PWM_TASK_PRIO);
    }
    osal_kthread_unlock();
}

/* Run the pwm_entry. */
app_run(pwm_entry);

```

## Precautions<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001664983942"></a>

-   Before calling the uapi\_pwm\_deinit interface, you need to call the uapi\_pwm\_close interface first.
-   The PWM supports a duty cycle of 0.
-   The PWM supports multi-channel complementary output.
-   After deep sleep wakeup, configure the multiplexing relationship first, then call the uapi\_pwm\_deinit interface to deinitialize, and then call the uapi\_pwm\_init interface for initialization.

# WDT<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713103557"></a>






## Overview<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713023501"></a>

WDT (Watch Dog Timer) is generally used to recover from exceptions when the CPU runs abnormally. If the system runs normally, the dog is fed regularly to prevent the timer from timing out. If the system stops running or fails to feed the dog normally for some reason, so that the timer is not reset within the set timeout period, the watchdog considers that the system has failed and triggers the corresponding handling measures, such as resetting the system or executing a specific error handling program.

The WDT specifications are as follows:

-   There is one CPU watchdog and one PMU watchdog. The PMU watchdog is not open to users.
-   The CPU watchdog timeout is adjustable from 2s to 32768s.
-   The CPU watchdog supports two working modes: direct reset and reset after interrupt.

## Function Description<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001664983938"></a>

The interfaces and functions provided by the WDT module are as follows:

-   uapi\_watchdog\_init（uint32\_t timeout）：Initializes the watchdog function and sets the watchdog timeout period in seconds（timeout indicates the watchdog timeout period）.
-   uapi\_watchdog\_deinit（void）：Deinitializes the watchdog function.
-   uapi\_watchdog\_set\_time（uint32\_t timeout）：Sets the watchdog timeout period in seconds (if not set, the default is 8s)（timeout indicates the watchdog timeout period）.
-   uapi\_watchdog\_kick（void）：Restarts the counter.
-   uapi\_watchdog\_enable（wdt\_mode\_t mode）：Enables the watchdog（mode indicates the watchdog mode）.
-   uapi\_watchdog\_disable（void）：Disables the watchdog.
-   uapi\_watchdog\_get\_left\_time（uint32\_t \*timeout）：Obtains the remaining time of the watchdog in ms（timeout indicates the remaining time value）.
-   uapi\_register\_watchdog\_callback（watchdog\_callback\_t callback）：Registers a watchdog callback（callback indicates the callback function that handles exceptions after the watchdog times out）.
-   uapi\_watchdog\_suspend（uintptr\_t arg）：Suspends the watchdog module for low-power scenarios（arg indicates the parameters required for suspension）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_WATCHDOG\_SUPPORT\_LPM macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_watchdog\_resume（uintptr\_t arg）：Resumes the watchdog module for low-power scenarios（arg indicates the parameters required for resume）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_WATCHDOG\_SUPPORT\_LPM macro is enabled.)

## Development Guide<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713103569"></a>

The WDT is generally used to detect whether the system hangs. If the dog is not fed within the configured feeding wait time, a system reset is generated or a dog interrupt is reported according to the watchdog enable mode. See the following reference code:

1.  Call uapi\_watchdog\_init to initialize and set the watchdog timeout period.
2.  Call uapi\_watchdog\_enable to enable the watchdog module. The reset mode and interrupt mode are configurable.
3.  Call uapi\_watchdog\_kick to feed the dog. At this point, the dog feeding operation has been implemented in the idle task.
4.  Call uapi\_watchdog\_get\_left\_time to obtain the remaining time of the watchdog timer (optional).
5.  Call uapi\_watchdog\_disable to disable the watchdog (it is not recommended to disable the watchdog under normal circumstances).

Example:

```
#include "watchdog.h"
#include "watchdog_porting.h"
void sample_wdt(void)
{
    uint32_t sample_remain_ms;
    /* Set the watchdog timeout period */
    uapi_watchdog_init(CHIP_WDT_TIMEOUT_32S);/* Set the timeout period */
    uapi_watchdog_enable(WDT_MODE_RESET);/* Enable the watchdog */
    osal_mdelay(5000); /* delay 5000 ms */
    uapi_watchdog_kick(); /* Feed the dog */
    uapi_watchdog_get_left_time(&sample_remain_ms); /* Obtain the remaining timeout period */
    osal_printk("sample_remain_ms = %x! \n", sample_remain_ms);
    uapi_watchdog_disable();/* Disable the watchdog */
}
```

## Precautions<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001664983970"></a>

-   If the obtained time is 0xFFFFFFFF, the watchdog is in the disabled state.
-   The watchdog has been enabled in the SDK and the dog feeding action already exists. In special scenarios, if the CPU needs to be occupied for a long time, you can disable the watchdog or add dog feeding operations in the business code to prevent the watchdog from resetting in normal business scenarios.
-   The default watchdog timeout period is 8s. In flashboot, the dog is reconfigured to 32s. Pay attention to this when using it. In general, it is not recommended to modify the timeout period.

# Timer<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713023573"></a>






## Overview<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001665143754"></a>

A Timer is an important module used for timing and generating timed events. It usually consists of a counter and some related registers. The core function of the timer is to count based on the configured clock source and the preset count value, and to generate an interrupt or trigger other events under specific conditions.

The Timer specifications are as follows:

-   4 timers (Timer0 to 3) are provided.
-   Each timer provides two 32-bit registers for counting.
-   Timeout interrupts and reload values are supported.

## Function Description<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001664983994"></a>

The interfaces and functions provided by the Timer module are as follows:

-   uapi\_timer\_adapter（timer\_index\_t index, uint32\_t int\_id, uint16\_t int\_priority）：Adapts the timer configuration（index indicates the hardware timer index，int\_id indicates the hardware timer interrupt ID，int\_priority indicates the hardware timer interrupt priority）.
-   uapi\_timer\_init（void）：Initializes the Timer.
-   uapi\_timer\_deinit（void）：Deinitializes the Timer.
-   uapi\_timer\_create（timer\_index\_t index, timer\_handle\_t \*timer）：Creates a timer（index indicates the hardware timer index，timer indicates the timer handle return value）.
-   uapi\_timer\_delete（timer\_handle\_t timer）：Deletes the specified timer（timer indicates the created timer）.
-   uapi\_timer\_start（timer\_handle\_t timer, uint32\_t time\_us, timer\_callback\_t callback, uintptr\_t data）：Starts the specified timer and begins timing（timer indicates the created timer，time\_us indicates the timer timeout period，callback indicates the timer callback function，data indicates the parameter passed to the timer callback function）.
-   uapi\_timer\_stop（timer\_handle\_t timer）：Stops the current timer timing（timer indicates the created timer）.
-   uapi\_timer\_get\_max\_us（void）：The user can obtain the maximum delay time (us) that the Timer can be set to.
-   uapi\_timer\_get\_current\_time\_us（timer\_index\_t index, uint32\_t \*current\_time\_us）：Obtains the current time (us) of the specified underlying Timer（index indicates the underlying Timer index，current\_time\_us indicates the current time in us of the underlying Timer）.
-   uapi\_timer\_start\_high\_precision（timer\_index\_t index, timer\_trigger\_mode\_t mode, uint32\_t time\_us, timer\_irq\_info\_t\* irq\_info, high\_precision\_timer\_callback\_t callback）：Starts a dedicated high-precision timer（index indicates the hardware timer index，mode indicates the timing mode，time\_us indicates the timer timeout period，irq\_info indicates the interrupt information，callback indicates the timer callback function）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_TIMER\_SUPPORT\_HIGH\_PRECISION macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_timer\_reset\_high\_precision（timer\_index\_t index, timer\_trigger\_mode\_t mode, uint32\_t time\_us）：Restarts a dedicated high-precision timer（index indicates the hardware timer index，mode indicates the timing mode，time\_us indicates the timer timeout period）.

    (It can be used only when the CONFIG\_TIMER\_SUPPORT\_HIGH\_PRECISION macro is enabled.)

-   uapi\_timer\_stop\_high\_precision（timer\_index\_t index）：Stops a dedicated high-precision timer（index indicates the hardware timer index）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_TIMER\_SUPPORT\_HIGH\_PRECISION macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_timer\_suspend（uintptr\_t val）：Suspends the timer module for low-power scenarios（val indicates the parameter required for suspension）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_TIMER\_SUPPORT\_LPM macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_timer\_resume（uintptr\_t val）：Resumes the timer module for low-power scenarios（val indicates the parameter required for resume）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_TIMER\_SUPPORT\_LPM macro is enabled.)

## Development Guide<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713103521"></a>

Use the Timer driver interface to create a timer that triggers an interrupt with a 5 ms period. See the following steps:

1.  Call the uapi\_timer\_adapter interface to configure the timer index, timer interrupt number, and interrupt priority.
2.  Call the uapi\_timer\_init interface to initialize the timer function.
3.  Call the uapi\_timer\_create interface to create a timer. The function parameter handle is the unique timer identifier.
4.  Call the uapi\_timer\_start interface to set the timeout period, timeout callback function, callback function input parameters, and start the timer.
5.  Call the uapi\_timer\_stop interface to stop the current timer timing.
6.  Call the uapi\_timer\_delete interface to delete the current timer.

Example:

```
#include "timer.h"
#define DELAY_5MS       5000
#define DELAY_1S        1000000
#define TIMER_IRQ_PRIO  3      /* Interrupt priority range, from high to low: 0~7 */
static timer_handle_t timer1_handle = 0;
static void timer1_callback(uintptr_t data);
void timer1_callback(uintptr_t data)
{
    unused(data);
    osal_printk("Timer1 5ms int test!\r\n");
    /* Enable the next timer interrupt */
    uapi_timer_start(timer1_handle, DELAY_5MS, timer1_callback, 0);
}
errcode_t test_timer_sample(void)
{
    errcode_t ret;
    /* Timer software initialization */
    uapi_timer_init();
    /* Configure timer1 hardware initialization, set the interrupt number, and configure the priority */
    ret = uapi_timer_adapter(TIMER_INDEX_1, TIMER_1_IRQN, TIMER_IRQ_PRIO);
    /* Create the timer1 software timer control handle */
    uapi_timer_create(TIMER_INDEX_1, &timer1_handle);
    /* Start the timer */
    uapi_timer_start(timer1_handle, DELAY_5MS, timer1_callback, 0);
    osal_mdelay(DELAY_1S);
    /* Stop the timer */
    uapi_timer_stop(timer1_handle);
    /* Delete the timer */
    uapi_timer_delete(timer1_handle);
    return ret;
}
```

## Precautions<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713023565"></a>

-   The timeout period of a timer created by the Timer is in μs.
-   By default, a maximum of 3 high-precision timers (Timer0 to 2) can be created at the same time.
-   Timer3 is used as the system clock source of LiteOS by default. Do not use the uapi interface to configure it.
-   In non-low-power mode, the maximum configurable timeout period of the Timer is about 4294s.
-   After confirming that the current high-precision timer is no longer needed, call the uapi\_timer\_delete interface to release the timer resources.
-   Do not call interfaces such as uapi\_timer\_stop and uapi\_timer\_delete in the Timer callback function.

# Systick<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001665143734"></a>






## Overview<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713103553"></a>

Systick is a hardware device or functional module in a microcontroller system, used to provide a precise time reference and timing function.

The system timing specifications are as follows:

-   Systick provides two 32-bit registers for storing count values, with a maximum count of 264-1.
-   An external 32.768 kHz crystal oscillator or an internal 32 kHz clock can be used as the clock source.

## Function Description<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713103509"></a>

The interfaces and functions provided by the Systick module are as follows:

-   uapi\_systick\_init（void）：Initializes the Systick.
-   uapi\_systick\_deinit（void）：Deinitializes the Systick.
-   uapi\_systick\_count\_clear（void）：Clears the Systick count.
-   uapi\_systick\_get\_count（void）：Obtains the Systick count value.
-   uapi\_systick\_get\_s（void）：Obtains the Systick count value in seconds.
-   uapi\_systick\_get\_ms（void）：Obtains the Systick count value in milliseconds.
-   uapi\_systick\_get\_us（void）：Obtains the Systick count value in microseconds.
-   uapi\_systick\_delay\_count（uint64\_t c\_delay）：Delays by count（c\_delay indicates the delay time）.
-   uapi\_systick\_delay\_s（uint32\_t s\_delay）：Delays by seconds（s\_delay indicates the delay time）.
-   uapi\_systick\_delay\_ms（uint32\_t m\_delay）：Delays by milliseconds（m\_delay indicates the delay time）.
-   uapi\_systick\_delay\_us（uint32\_t u\_delay）：Delays by microseconds（us\_delay indicates the delay time）.
-   uapi\_systick\_suspend（uintptr\_t arg）：Suspends the Systick module for low-power scenarios（arg indicates the parameters required for suspension）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_SYSTICK\_SUPPORT\_LPM macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_systick\_resume（uintptr\_t arg）：Resumes the Systick module for low-power scenarios（arg indicates the parameters required for resume）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_SYSTICK\_SUPPORT\_LPM macro is enabled.)

<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="14.398560143985604%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.6 "><p id="p165302711299"><a name="p165302711299"></a><a name="p165302711299"></a>Disabled by default. It is recommended to disable it.</p>
</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

## Development Guide<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001665143738"></a>

1.  Call the uapi\_systick\_init interface to initialize the Systick module.
2.  Call the uapi\_systick\_get\_count interface to obtain the current Systick count value.
3.  Call the uapi\_systick\_delay\_ms interface to delay the time passed in the input parameter.
4.  Call the uapi\_systick\_get\_count interface again to obtain the current Systick count value.

Example:

```
#include "systick.h"
void test_systick_sample(void)
{
    uint64_t count_before_delay_count;
    uint64_t count_after_delay_count;
    /* Initialize the systick module */
    uapi_systick_init();
    /* Verify the delay time through the difference of count values */
    count_before_delay_count= uapi_systick_get_count();
    uapi_systick_delay_ms(1000);
    count_after_delay_count = uapi_systick_get_count();
    osal_printk("test case delay count %lu.\r\n", count_before_delay_count - count_after_delay_count);
}
```

## Precautions<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001664984010"></a>

-   The Systick clock source uses the external 32.768k or internal 32k clock. The minimum counting unit is about 30μs. Pay attention when using the μs delay interface.
-   Systick is generally used to provide a stable clock signal as the reference clock of the entire microcontroller system. For high-precision delays, use TCXO.

# TCXO<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001664983986"></a>






## Overview<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001664984002"></a>

TCXO (Temperature Compensated Crystal Oscillator) is a temperature-compensated crystal oscillator. It reduces the impact of temperature on the oscillation frequency by introducing a temperature sensor and a temperature compensation circuit into the circuit, thereby providing a more stable clock signal. The chip has a built-in TCXO crystal oscillator and its counting unit, used for counting and delay. Users can also modify the clock configuration to use an external crystal oscillator as the clock input of the TCXO counting unit. The TCXO specifications are as follows:

-   The internal TCXO is up to 32 MHz, and the minimum counting unit is about 32 ns.
-   The TCXO counter provides two 32-bit registers for storing count values, with a maximum count of 2^64-1.

## Function Description<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001664983998"></a>

The interfaces and functions provided by the TCXO module are as follows:

-   uapi\_tcxo\_init（void）：Initializes the TCXO.
-   uapi\_tcxo\_deinit（void）：Deinitializes the TCXO.
-   uapi\_tcxo\_get\_count（void）：Obtains the TCXO count value.
-   uapi\_tcxo\_get\_ms（void）：Obtains the TCXO count value in milliseconds.
-   uapi\_tcxo\_get\_us（void）：Obtains the TCXO count value in microseconds.
-   uapi\_tcxo\_delay\_ms（uint32\_t m\_delay）：Sets the delay in milliseconds（m\_delay indicates the delay in milliseconds）.
-   uapi\_tcxo\_delay\_us（uint32\_t u\_delay）：Sets the delay in microseconds（u\_delay indicates the delay in microseconds）.
-   uapi\_tcxo\_suspend（uintptr\_t arg）：Suspends the TCXO module for low-power scenarios（arg indicates the parameters required for suspension）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_TCXO\_SUPPORT\_LPM macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_tcxo\_resume（uintptr\_t arg）：Resumes the TCXO module for low-power scenarios（arg indicates the parameters required for resume）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_TCXO\_SUPPORT\_LPM macro is enabled.)

## Development Guide<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001665143770"></a>

1.  Call the uapi\_tcxo\_init interface to initialize the TCXO module.
2.  Call the uapi\_tcxo\_get\_count interface to obtain the current TCXO count value.
3.  Call the uapi\_tcxo\_delay\_ms interface to delay the time passed in the input parameter.

Example:

```
#include "tcxo.h"
void test_tcxo_sample(void)
{
    uint64_t count_before_delay_count;
    uint64_t count_after_delay_count;
    /* Initialize the tcxo module */
    uapi_tcxo_init();
    /* Verify the delay time through the difference of count values */
    count_before_delay_count = uapi_tcxo_get_count();
    uapi_tcxo_delay_ms(1000);
    count_after_delay_count= uapi_tcxo_get_count();
    osal_printk("test case delay count %lu.\r\n", count_before_delay_count - count_after_delay_count);
    return;
}
```

## Precautions<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713023557"></a>

None.

# Flash<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001664983962"></a>






## Overview<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001664983978"></a>

Flash is a non-volatile flash memory technology, also called flash memory, which usually supports the SPI protocol. Flash can implement various commands such as read, write, and erase through the SPI protocol.

## Function Description<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001664983990"></a>

The interfaces and functions provided by the Flash module are as follows:

-   uapi\_flash\_init（flash\_id\_t id）：Initializes the Flash（id indicates the flash id）.
-   uapi\_flash\_deinit（flash\_id\_t id）：Deinitializes the Flash（id indicates the flash id）.
-   uapi\_flash\_write\_data（flash\_id\_t id, uint32\_t addr, const uint8\_t \*src, uint32\_t length）：Writes data to the Flash（id indicates the flash id，addr indicates the flash write address，src indicates the data address，length indicates the data length）.
-   uapi\_flash\_write\_data\_without\_align（flash\_id\_t id, uint32\_t addr, const uint8\_t \*src, uint32\_t length）：Writes data to the Flash at an unaligned address（id indicates the flash id，addr indicates the flash write address，src indicates the data address，length indicates the data length）.
-   uapi\_flash\_read\_data（flash\_id\_t id, uint32\_t addr, uint8\_t \*dst, uint32\_t length）：Reads data from the Flash（id indicates the flash id，addr indicates the flash read address，dst indicates the data address，length indicates the data length）.
-   uapi\_flash\_read\_data\_without\_align（flash\_id\_t id, uint32\_t addr, uint8\_t \*dst, uint32\_t length）：Reads data from the Flash at an unaligned address（id indicates the flash id，addr indicates the flash read address，dst indicates the data address，length indicates the data length）.
-   uapi\_flash\_chip\_erase（flash\_id\_t id）：Erases the data of the entire Flash chip（id indicates the flash id）.
-   uapi\_flash\_block\_erase（flash\_id\_t id, uint32\_t addr, uint32\_t length, bool is\_wait）：Erases Flash data by block（id indicates the flash id，addr indicates the flash erase address，length indicates the erase length，is\_wait indicates whether to wait for the erase to complete）.
-   uapi\_flash\_sector\_erase（flash\_id\_t id, uint32\_t addr, bool is\_wait）：Erases Flash data by sector（id indicates the flash id，addr indicates the flash erase address，is\_wait indicates whether to wait for the erase to complete）.
-   uapi\_flash\_suspend（flash\_id\_t id）：Puts the Flash to sleep（id indicates the flash id）.
-   uapi\_flash\_resume（flash\_id\_t id）：Wakes up the Flash from sleep（id indicates the flash id）.
-   uapi\_flash\_switch\_to\_deeppower（flash\_id\_t id）：Makes the Flash enter deep power-down mode（id indicates the flash id）.
-   uapi\_flash\_resume\_from\_deeppower（flash\_id\_t id）：Wakes up the Flash from deep power-down mode（id indicates the flash id）.
-   uapi\_flash\_read\_security\_status（flash\_id\_t id, uint8\_t \*read\_data）：Reads the Flash security register status（id indicates the flash id，read\_data indicates the read data）.
-   uapi\_flash\_send\_cmd\_exe（flash\_id\_t id, flash\_cmd\_exe\_t \*cmd\_exe）：Sends a command to the Flash（id indicates the flash id，cmd\_exe indicates the flash command）.
-   uapi\_flash\_get\_info（flash\_id\_t id, flash\_info\_t \*flash\_info）：Obtains the current Flash information（id indicates the flash id，flash\_info indicates the flash information）.
-   uapi\_flash\_reset（flash\_id\_t id）：Resets the Flash（id indicates the flash id）.
-   uapi\_flash\_is\_processing（flash\_id\_t id）：Checks whether the Flash is working（id indicates the flash id）.
-   uapi\_flash\_read\_id（flash\_id\_t id, uint32\_t \*manufacture\_id）：Reads the Flash manufacturer ID（id indicates the flash id，manufacture\_id indicates the flash manufacturer id）.
-   uapi\_flash\_read\_unique\_id\(flash\_id\_t id, uint8\_t \*unique\_id：Reads the Flash unique ID（id indicates the flash id，manufacture\_id indicates the flash unique id）.
-   uapi\_flash\_read\_device\_id（flash\_id\_t id, uint16\_t \*device\_id）：Reads the Flash device ID（id indicates the flash id，device\_id indicates the flash device id）.
-   uapi\_flash\_read\_status（flash\_id\_t id, uint8\_t \*status）：Reads the Flash status and waits for idle（id indicates the flash id，status indicates the flash status）.
-   uapi\_flash\_save\_manufacture（flash\_id\_t id, uint32\_t manufacture\_id）：Saves the Flash manufacturer ID（id indicates the flash id，manufacture\_id indicates the flash manufacturer id）.
-   uapi\_flash\_set\_spi\_baud（flash\_id\_t id, uint32\_t bus\_clk, uint32\_t freq\_mhz）：Sets the SPI baud rate（id indicates the flash id，bus\_clk indicates the bus clock，freq\_mhz indicates the SPI working clock frequency）.

## Development Guide<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001664984022"></a>

The development and adaptation of the external Flash must be selected and adapted by the customer. Part of the reference code is in the files: drivers/chips/bs2x/porting/flash/flash\_porting.c and drivers/chips/bs2x/main\_init/main\_init.c

```
/* drivers/chips/bs2x/porting/flash/flash_porting.c */
static flash_cfg_t g_flash_porting_config[FLASH_MAX] = {
    {
        .isinit = 0,
        .flash_manufacturer = FLASH_MANUFACTURER_MAX,
        .unique_id = 0,
        .bus = SPI_BUS_1,
        .mode = HAL_SPI_FRAME_FORMAT_STANDARD,
        .is_xip = false,
        .attr = {
            .is_slave = false,
            .slave_num = 1,
            .bus_clk = SPI_CLK_FREQ,
            .freq_mhz = 4,
            .clk_polarity = (uint32_t)SPI_CFG_CLK_CPOL_0,
            .clk_phase = (uint32_t)SPI_CFG_CLK_CPHA_0,
            .frame_format = SPI_CFG_FRAME_FORMAT_MOTOROLA_SPI,
            .spi_frame_format = (uint32_t)HAL_SPI_FRAME_FORMAT_STANDARD,
            .frame_size = (uint32_t)HAL_SPI_FRAME_SIZE_8,
            .sste = SPI_CFG_SSTE_DISABLE,
        },
        .extra_attr = {
            .tx_use_dma = false,
            .rx_use_dma = false,
            .qspi_param = { 0 },
        },
    },
};

/* drivers/chips/bs2x/main_init/main_init.c */
static void ota_extern_flash_init(void)
{
#if defined(SUPPORT_EXTERN_FLASH)
    flash_porting_pinmux_cfg(0);
    uint32_t manufacture_id = 0;
    uapi_flash_init(0);
    uapi_flash_read_id(0, &manufacture_id);
    flash_save_manufacturer(0, manufacture_id);
#endif
}

```

**To adapt a newly selected external Flash, you need to maintain the global variable g\_flash\_device\_parameter in drivers/chips/bs2x/porting/flash/flash\_config\_info\_porting.h. The structure type of this global variable is flash\_device\_parameter\_t:**

```
typedef struct flash_device_parameter {
    uint32_t manufacturer_id;                               /*!< @if Eng Flash manufacturer id.
    @else Flash manufacturer id.@endif */
    uint32_t flash_size;                                    /*!< @if Eng Flash size.
    @else Flash size.@endif */
    uint8_t security_reg_cmd;                               /*!< @if Eng Security reg cmd.
    @else Security register command.@endif */
    uint8_t suspend_cmd;                                    /*!< @if Eng Suspend cmd.
    @else Suspend command.@endif */
    uint8_t resume_cmd;                                     /*!< @if Eng Resume cmd.
    @else Resume command.@endif *
}
```

-   manufacturer\_id: It can be read in single-line mode. Send 0x9F to read the id, look it up in the manual, and compare whether the results obtained by the two methods are consistent. The manufacturer\_id locations of some common manufacturers are listed below.

    ![](figures/zh-cn_image_0000002295194265.png)

    ![](figures/zh-cn_image_0000002295082025.png)

    ![](figures/zh-cn_image_0000002295223829.png)

    ```
    uint32_t manufacture_id;
    uapi_flash_init(FLASH_0);
    uapi_flash_read_id(FLASH_0, &manufacture_id);
    ```

-   flash\_size: The total Flash space is usually introduced in Chapter 1

    ![](figures/zh-cn_image_0000002260650652.png)

    ![](figures/zh-cn_image_0000002260655218.png)

    ![](figures/zh-cn_image_0000002260657558.png)

-   security\_reg\_cmd: The command to read the Flash status register. Search for Read Security Registers in the manual. It can be found in the Commands Table.
-   suspend\_cmd and resume\_cmd: For Flash that supports sleep, search for suspend and resume in the manual. They can be found in the Commands Table.

    **drivers/chips/bs2x/porting/flash/flash\_config\_info\_porting.h also maintains a global variable g\_flash\_need\_set\_qe. This global variable stores the manufacturer id of the Flash that needs to enable the QE bit before enabling quad-line access, and the operation to enable the QE bit. Whether the QE bit needs to be enabled can be determined by searching for Quad Enable \(QE\) in the manual.**

## Precautions<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713023545"></a>

-   XIP mode is not supported.
-   When performing read, write, or erase operations on the Flash, ensure that the Flash has been initialized.
-   Customers need to adapt based on the Flash selection.

# SFC<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001664984018"></a>






## Overview<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713023581"></a>

SFC (SPI Flash Controller) is an AHB SPI Flash controller. The Flash mounted on the SFC module can be operated by accessing the SFC mapped address range as an AHB Slave. It also supports accessing the mounted Flash through the APB Slave method in a form similar to traditional SPI.

## Function Description<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713103573"></a>

-   uapi\_sfc\_init（sfc\_flash\_config\_t \*config）: Initializes the SFC module. The configuration parameters include the configuration of the mapped address space and the read/write form（config indicates the initialization parameters）.
-   uapi\_sfc\_init\_rom（sfc\_flash\_config\_t \*config）: Initializes the SFC module in compatibility mode. In this interface, only the mapping\_addr parameter takes effect, and the read/write forms use the common Flash read (0x03) and write (0x02) commands. This configuration is also applied when the return value of uapi\_sfc\_init is ERRCODE\_SFC\_FLASH\_NOT\_SUPPORT (0x80001341)（config indicates the initialization parameters）.
-   uapi\_sfc\_deinit（void）: Deinitializes the SFC driver.
-   uapi\_sfc\_reg\_read（uint32\_t flash\_addr, uint8\_t \*read\_buffer, uint32\_t read\_size）: Provides the read function in register mode. The read data is stored in read\_buffer byte by byte. Do not call it in an interrupt（flash\_addr indicates the first address of the Flash where the data resides，read\_buffer indicates the buffer used to receive data，read\_size indicates the number of bytes to read）.
-   uapi\_sfc\_reg\_write（uint32\_t flash\_addr, uint8\_t \*write\_data, uint32\_t write\_size）: Provides the write function in register mode. The data to be written is stored in write\_data byte by byte. Do not call it in an interrupt（flash\_addr indicates the first address of the target Flash，write\_data indicates the data to be written，write\_size indicates the number of bytes of the written data）.
-   uapi\_sfc\_reg\_erase（uint32\_t flash\_addr, uint32\_t erase\_size）: Erases the Flash in register mode. When write-back is not enabled, the address and size are required to be aligned by sector. Do not call it in an interrupt（flash\_addr indicates the first address to erase，erase\_size indicates the size of the Flash space to erase）.
-   uapi\_sfc\_reg\_erase\_chip（void）: Erases the entire Flash chip in register mode. Do not call it in an interrupt.
-   uapi\_sfc\_regs\_other\_flash\_opt（sfc\_flash\_op\_t cmd\_type, uint8\_t cmd, uint8\_t \*buffer, uint32\_t length）: Reads and writes Flash attributes in register mode. Do not call it in an interrupt（cmd\_type indicates the read/write type of the setting command，cmd indicates the SPI command，buffer indicates the data buffer，length indicates the length of the data to read/write，and its value must be less than 4）.
-   uapi\_sfc\_lock\_protect（sfc\_flash\_protect\_region\_t region）: Enables the SFC write protection（region indicates the Flash protected region）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_SFC\_SUPPORT\_WRITE\_PROTECT macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_sfc\_unlock\_protect（void）: Encapsulated interface to disable the Flash write protection.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_SFC\_SUPPORT\_WRITE\_PROTECT macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_sfc\_dma\_write（uint32\_t flash\_addr, uint8\_t \*write\_buffer, uint32\_t write\_size）：Provides the DMA mode write function. The data to be written is stored in write\_buffer byte by byte. Do not call it in an interrupt（flash\_addr indicates the first address of the target Flash，write\_buffer indicates the data to be written，write\_size indicates the number of bytes of the written data）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_SFC\_SUPPORT\_DMA macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_sfc\_dma\_read（uint32\_t flash\_addr, uint8\_t \*read\_buffer, uint32\_t read\_size）：Provides the DMA mode read function. The read data is stored in read\_buffer byte by byte. Do not call it in an interrupt（flash\_addr indicates the first address of the Flash where the data resides，read\_buffer indicates the buffer used to receive data，read\_size indicates the number of bytes to read）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_SFC\_SUPPORT\_DMA macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_sfc\_suspend（uintptr\_t arg）：Suspends the SFC module for low-power scenarios（arg indicates the parameters required for suspension）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_SFC\_SUPPORT\_LPM macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_sfc\_resume（uintptr\_t arg）：Resumes the SFC module for low-power scenarios（arg indicates the parameters required for resume）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_SFC\_SUPPORT\_LPM macro is enabled.)

<td class="cellrowborder" valign="top" width="14.3985601439856%" headers="mcps1.2.7.1.6 "><p id="p1762719286324"><a name="p1762719286324"></a><a name="p1762719286324"></a>Disabled by default. Enable it as appropriate.</p>
</td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>

-   SFC\_SUPPORT\_DMA: The SFC specification does not support APB\_MASTER DMA access, so the uapi\_sfc\_dma\_xxx interfaces under this definition are unavailable.
-   SFC\_ALLOW\_ERASE\_WRITEBACK：The erase interface performs automatic write-back internally, requiring 8K of DTCM space as global variables. It is not recommended to enable it for the specification of this product.
-   SFC\_SUPPORT\_LPM: Supports SFC low-power field save and restore. The low-power process inside the SDK has been implemented by default. When debugging low power consumption, be sure to enable this configuration item.
-   SFC\_SUPPORT\_WRITE\_PROTECT：Enables the write protection interface encapsulated inside the SDK.
-   SFC\_USE\_CUSTOMIZED\_DEVICE\_INFO: When enabled, the custom configuration group adapted in the porting layer is used. When disabled, the default adapted configuration items in include/driver/sfc/sfc\_config\_info.h inside the SDK are used. The co-packaged Flash has been adapted in the custom configuration group in the SDK. Enable this macro to use it.

## Development Guide<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713103541"></a>

The SDK initializes the SFC driver by default. When using the APB Slave interface, no initialization is required. The sample code is as follows:

```
#include "soc_osal.h"
#include "securec.h"
#include "sfc.h"
#include "sfc_porting.h"
#include "app_init.h"
#include "memory_config_common.h"

#define SFC_TASK_PRIO                   24
#define SFC_TASK_STACK_SIZE             0x1000
#define SFC_SAMPLE_LEN                  0x80000
#define SFC_PRINT_BUFF_LEN              32
uint8_t g_print_data_buff[SFC_PRINT_BUFF_LEN] = {0};
uint8_t g_write_data_buff[SFC_PRINT_BUFF_LEN] = {0};

static void sfc_sample_start_api_test(void)
{
    osal_printk("API test start\r\n");
    uint32_t remained_len = CONFIG_SFC_SAMPLE_USER_SIZE;
    uint32_t start_addr = CONFIG_SFC_SAMPLE_USER_ADDR;
    while (remained_len > 0) {
        uint32_t cur_len = remained_len > SFC_PRINT_BUFF_LEN ? SFC_PRINT_BUFF_LEN : remained_len;
        uapi_sfc_reg_read(start_addr, g_print_data_buff, cur_len);
        for (uint8_t i = 0; i < cur_len; i++) {
            osal_printk("%02x ", g_print_data_buff[i]);
        }
        uapi_sfc_reg_write(start_addr, g_write_data_buff, cur_len);
        start_addr += cur_len;
        remained_len -= cur_len;
        osal_printk("\r\n");
    }
    start_addr = CONFIG_SFC_SAMPLE_USER_ADDR;
    remained_len = CONFIG_SFC_SAMPLE_USER_SIZE;
    while (remained_len > 0) {
        uint32_t cur_len = remained_len > SFC_PRINT_BUFF_LEN ? SFC_PRINT_BUFF_LEN : remained_len;
        uapi_sfc_reg_read(start_addr, g_print_data_buff, cur_len);
        for (uint8_t i = 0; i < cur_len; i++) {
            osal_printk("%02x ", g_print_data_buff[i]);
        }
        start_addr += cur_len;
        remained_len -= cur_len;
        osal_printk("\r\n");
    }
}

static void *sfc_task(const char *arg)
{
    unused(arg);
    for (uint8_t i = 0; i < SFC_PRINT_BUFF_LEN; i++) {
        g_write_data_buff[i] = i;
    }
    /* Erase User space */
    osal_printk("Erasing for API sample...\r\n");
    errcode_t ret = uapi_sfc_reg_erase(CONFIG_SFC_SAMPLE_USER_ADDR, CONFIG_SFC_SAMPLE_USER_SIZE);
    if (ret != ERRCODE_SUCC) {
        osal_printk("flash erase failed! ret = %x\r\n", ret);
        return NULL;
    }
    osal_printk("Start API read sample...\r\n");
    sfc_sample_start_api_test();
    return NULL;
}

static void sfc_entry(void)
{
    osal_task *task_handle = NULL;
    osal_kthread_lock();
    task_handle = osal_kthread_create((osal_kthread_handler)sfc_task, 0, "SFCTask", SFC_TASK_STACK_SIZE);
    if (task_handle != NULL) {
        osal_kthread_set_priority(task_handle, SFC_TASK_PRIO);
        osal_kfree(task_handle);
    }
    osal_kthread_unlock();
}

```

## Precautions<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001665143742"></a>

-   The SFC-related code must be placed in the ITCM. The SFC driver of this product is the only means to maintain the XIP execution chain.
-   During XIP execution, the instruction fetch performance will be reduced by 10 to 60 times (depending on the Flash performance and aging degree). Therefore, drivers with performance requirements must not be placed on the Flash for XIP execution.
-   For the uapi-form APB slave interfaces provided by the driver, the passed address is the real Flash address rather than the mapped address.
-   According to chip limitations, it is strictly forbidden to write the co-packaged Flash by directly accessing the mapped address.

# QDEC<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001665143694"></a>





## Overview<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713103549"></a>

QDEC (Quadrature Decoder) is a quadrature encoder that provides buffered decoding of quadrature encoder sensor signals. The encoder has 2 monitoring points with a 90° phase difference, which means that the level of one monitoring point always changes before that of the other. When the wheel moves one notch, the 2 monitoring points each detect one pulse. When the quadrature encoder starts, the decoder continuously samples the two input waveforms and compares the current sample \(n\) with the previous sample \(n-1\) for decoding. The comparison results are shown in [Table 1](#table1934195705217).

**Table 1**  QDEC input waveform sampling result comparison

## Function Description<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001664983966"></a>

The interfaces and functions provided by the QDEC module are as follows:

-   uapi\_qdec\_init（qdec\_config\_t const \*config）：Initializes the QDEC module（config indicates the initialization configuration parameters）.
-   uapi\_qdec\_deinit（void）：Deinitializes the QDEC.
-   uapi\_qdec\_enable（void）：Enables the QDEC.
-   uapi\_qdec\_disable（void）：Disables the QDEC.
-   uapi\_qdec\_read\_accumulators（int16\_t \*acc, int16\_t \*accdbl）：Reads the QDEC accumulation registers（acc indicates the accumulated number of effective conversions registered，accdbl indicates the accumulated number of repeated conversions detected and registered）.
-   uapi\_qdec\_register\_callback（qdec\_callback\_t callback）：Registers the QDEC interrupt callback function（callback indicates the QDEC interrupt callback function）.
-   qdec\_port\_set\_acc\_per\_roll\(uint8\_t acc\_per\_roll\)：Configures the value added to the accumulator when the wheel rolls once. It is applicable to relatively special grating sensors.

## Development Guide<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713023513"></a>

Example:

1.  First, call the qdec\_port\_pinmux\_init interface to configure the IO as a QDEC function through multiplexing.
2.  Then, call the uapi\_qdec\_init interface to initialize the QDEC. The configurable parameters during initialization are shown in the structure.

    Input parameter structure of uapi\_qdec\_init:

    ```
    typedef struct {
        hal_qdec_report_per_t reportper;            /*!< @if Eng Report period in samples.
                                                         @else   Sample reporting period @endif */
        hal_qdec_sample_per_t sampleper;            /*!< @if Eng Sampling period in microseconds.
                                                         @else   Sampling period (in microseconds) @endif */
        hal_qdec_defen_num_t  defen_num;            /*!< @if Eng Debounce num.
                                                         @else   Debounce period @endif */
        uint32_t              psela;                /*!< @if Eng Pin number for A input.
                                                         @else   Pin number of input A @endif */
        uint32_t              pselb;                /*!< @if Eng Pin number for B input.
                                                         @else   Pin number of input B @endif */
        uint32_t              pselled;              /*!< @if Eng Pin number for LED output.
                                                         @else   Pin number of LED output @endif */
        uint32_t              ledpre;               /*!< @if Eng Time of LED is switched on before sampling in microseconds
                                                         @else   Time of LED switched on before sampling (in microseconds) @endif */
        hal_qdec_led_pol_t    ledpol;               /*!< @if Eng Active LED polarity.
                                                         @else   Active LED polarity @endif */
        bool                  defence;              /*!< @if Eng Switch of debouncing filter.
                                                         @else   Switch of the debounce filter function @endif */
        bool                  sample_inten;         /*!< @if Eng Enabling sample ready interrupt.
                                                         @else   Enable the sample-ready interrupt @endif */
        uint8_t               interrupt_priority;   /*!< @if Eng QDEC interrupt priority.
                                                         @else   QDEC interrupt priority @endif */
    } qdec_config_t;
    #define QDEC_DEFAULT_CONFIG                                              \
    {                                                                        \
        .reportper           = QDEC_REPORTPER_1,                             \
        .sampleper           = QDEC_SAMPLEPER_512US,                         \
        .defen_num           = QDEC_DEBOUNCE_NUM_2,                          \
        .psela               = QDEC_CONFIG_PIO_A,                            \
        .pselb               = QDEC_CONFIG_PIO_B,                            \
        .pselled             = QDEC_CONFIG_PIO_LED,                          \
        .ledpre              = 256,                                          \
        .ledpol              = QDEC_LED_POL_ACTIVE_HIGH,                     \
        .defence             = true,                                         \
        .sample_inten        = false,                                        \
        .interrupt_priority  = IRQ_QDEC,                                     \
    }
    ```

3.  Call the uapi\_qdec\_register\_callback and uapi\_qdec\_enable interfaces to register the callback function for QDEC interrupt triggering and enable the QDEC.

    ```
    static qdec_config_t g_qdec_config = QDEC_DEFAULT_CONFIG;
    static int32_t qdec_report_callback(int argc, char *argv[])
    {
        UNUSED(argv);
        g_qdec_count += argc;
        printf("curren count is: %d. \r\n", g_qdec_count);
        return ERRCODE_SUCC;
    }
    int32_t test_qdec_open(void)
    {
        uapi_qdec_init(&g_qdec_config);
        uapi_qdec_register_callback(qdec_report_callback);
        uapi_qdec_enable();
        return TEST_SUITE_OK;
    }
    ```

## Precautions<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713103565"></a>

If the grounding is wrong, double-scroll errors or false scroll reports are likely to occur. During the first initialization, the pins may generate level changes, causing one double-scroll error to be reported. This error can be ignored.

# KEYSCAN<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001665143750"></a>






## Overview<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001665143774"></a>

KEYSCAN is a keyboard scanning module that supports row scan and column scan modes, with a maximum of a 16 x 8 matrix keyboard. Unused pins can be disabled by configuring registers. It supports the chip algorithm for ghost key prevention and supports a debounce design where IO ports can be bypassed.

## Function Description<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713023529"></a>

The interfaces and functions provided by the KEYSCAN module are as follows:

-   uapi\_keyscan\_init（keyscan\_pulse\_time\_t time, keyscan\_mode\_t mode, keyscan\_int\_t event\_type）：Initializes KEYSCAN（time indicates the Keyscan scan duration，mode indicates the Keyscan scan mode，event\_type indicates the Keyscan interrupt type）.
-   uapi\_keyscan\_deinit（void）：Deinitializes KEYSCAN.
-   uapi\_keyscan\_enable（void）：Enables KEYSCAN.
-   uapi\_keyscan\_disable（void）：Disables KEYSCAN.
-   uapi\_keyscan\_register\_callback（keyscan\_report\_callback\_t callback）：Registers a KEYSCAN callback（callback indicates the Keyscan callback function）.
-   uapi\_keyscan\_unregister\_callback（keyscan\_report\_callback\_t callback）：Deregisters a KEYSCAN callback（callback indicates the Keyscan callback function）.
-   uapi\_set\_keyscan\_value\_map（uint8\_t \*\*map\_addr, uint16\_t row, uint8\_t col）：Registers the KEYSCAN key-value map（map\_addr indicates the key-value matrix address，row indicates the number of rows of the key-value matrix，col indicates the number of columns of the key-value matrix）.
-   uapi\_keyscan\_suspend（uintptr\_t arg）：Suspends all Keyscan channels for low-power scenarios（arg indicates the parameters required for suspension）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_KEYSCAN\_SUPPORT\_LPM macro is enabled.)
-   uapi\_keyscan\_resume（uintptr\_t arg）：Resumes all Keyscan channels for low-power scenarios（arg indicates the parameters required for resume）.(It can be used only when the CONFIG\_KEYSCAN\_SUPPORT\_LPM macro is enabled.)

## Development Guide<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713103513"></a>

The configurable items are mainly in the porting-layer header file corresponding to KEYSCAN. The following describes the functions of important configurable items.

```
#define KEYSCAN_DEFAULT_CONFIG                       \
{                                                    \
.direction             = ROW_SCAN_DIR,               \    Row/column scan direction
.scan_mode             = HAL_KEYSCAN_MODE_0,         \    Row/column scan mode
.pulse_time            = EVERY_ROW_PULSE_10_US,      \   Scan pulse duration
.wait_time             = WAITING_30_AND_5_US,        \  Scan duration
.idle_time             = IDLE_3_MS_AFTER_SCAN,       \  Wait duration between multiple scans
.defence_time          = SAMPLE_3TIMES_DEFENCE,      \   Debounce count
.ghost_check           = false,                      \Hardware ghost prevention switch
.io_de                = true,                      \
}
//  Configuration of the frequency division ratio of the two-stage keyscan frequency divider. The division coefficient is CLK_HIGH_DIV*CLK_LOW_DIV. The default configuration is
#define CLK_HIGH_DIV 0x28
#define CLK_LOW_DIV  0xa
//  To reduce the division ratio to optimize the keyscan delay, the recommended configuration is 8M, that is,
#define CLK_HIGH_DIV 0x2
#define CLK_LOW_DIV  0x2
```

Example:

1.  If the custom mode is used, first call the keyscan\_porting\_set\_gpio interface to confirm the gpio to use. Write row first, then column.

    ```
    #if defined(CONFIG_KEYSCAN_USER_CONFIG_TYPE)
        uint8_t user_gpio_map[CONFIG_KEYSCAN_ENABLE_ROW + CONFIG_KEYSCAN_ENABLE_COL] = { 31, 24, 14, 23, 27, 28, 10, 11, 30, 13, 15, 16, 25, 26, 12, 22, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 21, 9, 29};
    if (keyscan_porting_set_gpio(user_gpio_map)) {
    return;
    }
    #endif
    ```

2.  Call the uapi\_set\_keyscan\_value\_map interface to register the key-value map. The rows and columns of the key-value map array must be consistent with the maximum row and column values. Fill unused keys with 0x00.

    ```
    uint8_t g_key_map_test[KEY_SCAN_MAX_ROW][KEY_SCAN_MAX_COL] = {
        { 0x29, 0x2B, 0x14, 0x35, 0x04, 0x1E, 0x1D, 0x00 },
        { 0x3D, 0x3C, 0x08, 0x3B, 0x07, 0x20, 0x06, 0x00 },
        { 0x00, 0x39, 0x1A, 0x3A, 0x16, 0x1F, 0x1B, 0x00 },
        { 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0xE0, 0x00, 0x00, 0xE4, 0x00 },
        { 0x0A, 0x17, 0x15, 0x22, 0x09, 0x21, 0x19, 0x05 },
        { 0x0B, 0x1C, 0x18, 0x23, 0x0D, 0x24, 0x10, 0x11 },
        { 0x3F, 0x30, 0x0C, 0x2E, 0x0E, 0x25, 0x36, 0x00 },
        { 0x00, 0x00, 0x12, 0x40, 0x0F, 0x26, 0x37, 0x00 },
        { 0x34, 0x2F, 0x13, 0x2D, 0x33, 0x27, 0x00, 0x38 },
        { 0x3E, 0x2A, 0x00, 0x41, 0x31, 0x42, 0x28, 0x2C },
        { 0x00, 0x00, 0xE3, 0x00, 0x00, 0x43, 0x00, 0x51 },
        { 0xE2, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x45, 0xE5, 0xE6 },
        { 0x00, 0x53, 0x00, 0x00, 0xE1, 0x44, 0x00, 0x4F },
        { 0x52, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x4C, 0x50 },
        { 0x5F, 0x5C, 0x61, 0x5E, 0x59, 0x62, 0x55, 0x5B },
        { 0x54, 0x60, 0x56, 0x57, 0x5D, 0x5A, 0x58, 0x63 },
    };
    uapi_set_keyscan_value_map((uint8_t **)g_key_map_test, KEY_SCAN_MAX_ROW, KEY_SCAN_MAX_COL);
    ```

3.  Call the uapi\_keyscan\_init interface to initialize KEYSCAN. The current input parameters are the pulse time (param1), scan mode (param2), and interrupt time (param3). The default values are 0, 0, 3.

    ```
    uapi_keyscan_init(pulse, mode, scan_int);
    ```

4.  Call the uapi\_keyscan\_register\_callback and uapi\_keyscan\_enable interfaces to register the KEYSCAN interrupt callback function and enable KEYSCAN.

    ```
    int test_key_scan_funcs(void)
    {
        errcode_t ret = ERRCODE_FAIL;
        ret = uapi_keyscan_register_callback(key_scan_report_callback);
        if (ret != ERRCODE_SUCC) {
            return TEST_SUITE_TEST_FAILED;
        }
        uapi_keyscan_enable();
        return TEST_SUITE_OK;
    }
    ```

**Full-key anti-ghosting mode<a name="section17811101414518"></a>**

After CONFIG\_KEYSCAN\_ENABLE\_REP\_ALL\_KEY is enabled, the interrupt report is replaced with keyscan\_porting\_all\_keys\_report, which reports all keys pressed at the same time. When a key is held down, it is reported once per scan cycle. Users need to adjust the interrupt response threshold according to the scan frequency.

**Debounce mode<a name="section11396202710511"></a>**

After the CONFIG\_KEYSCAN\_SUPPORT\_SW\_DEFENCE macro is enabled, configure an idle pin to CONFIG\_KEYSCAN\_ANOTHER\_COL and increase the number of column pins by 1. Then, no false key reports under slight jitter will occur during keyscan execution. Because enabling the key debounce function occupies one gpio pin, the CONFIG\_KEYSCAN\_SUPPORT\_SW\_DEFENCE macro is disabled by default and needs to be configured by users according to the actual situation.

**Software ghost prevention mode<a name="section48517365510"></a>**

BS2X provides the software ghost key prevention function. After CONFIG\_KEYSCAN\_SOFTWARE\_GHOST\_DETECT is enabled, keyscan\_sw\_ghost\_detect is added to the Keyscan callback function. The third key that forms the ghost key and the fourth key reported by the ghost key are deleted from the reporting buffer, and only the initially pressed two keys are reported. Meanwhile, other newly pressed keys are no longer reported, so as to inform users that a ghost key phenomenon has occurred. The software ghost prevention function cannot be enabled together with hardware ghost prevention.

## Precautions<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713103489"></a>

When using KEYSCAN, ensure proper grounding. Otherwise, false key-value reports may occur.

# PDM<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713023521"></a>






## Overview<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713023541"></a>

PDM (Pulse Density Modulation) is a modulation method that uses digital signals to represent analog signals. Another commonly used method for converting analog quantities to digital quantities, PCM, uses equally spaced sampling to represent the amplitude of the analog component of each sample as an N-bit digital component (N = quantization depth). Therefore, in the PCM mode, the result of each sample is data with an N-bit word length. PDM samples and modulates the analog component using a clock much higher than the PCM sampling rate. When there is only a 1-bit output, it is 0 or 1. Therefore, digital audio represented by the PDM mode is also called oversampled 1-bit audio.

The audio digital-to-analog conversion module carried by 282X samples using the PDM method and **outputs PCM data** after processing. The data source can be DMIC or AMIC.

## Function Description<a name="ZH-CN_TOPIC_0000001713023569"></a>

The interfaces and functions provided by the PDM module are as follows:

-   uapi\_pdm\_init（void）：Initializes the PDM.
-   uapi\_pdm\_deinit（void）：Deinitializes the PDM.
-   uapi\_pdm\_set\_attr（uint8\_t mic, const pdm\_config\_t \*attr）：Sets the attributes of a PDM channel（mic indicates the channel to configure，attr indicates the configuration parameters）.
-   uapi\_pdm\_get\_attr（uint8\_t mic, pdm\_config\_t \*attr）：Obtains the attributes of a PDM channel（mic indicates the channel to obtain，attr indicates the attribute parameters used to store the obtained values）.
-   uapi\_pdm\_start（void）：Starts PDM sampling.
-   uapi\_pdm\_stop（void）：Stops PDM sampling.
-   uapi\_pdm\_get\_fifo\_addr（void）：Obtains the FIFO base address of the PDM.
-   uapi\_pdm\_get\_fifo\_deepth（void）：Obtains the FIFO depth of the PDM.
